# Components

Detail reference for `src/components/` (workspace, task, overlays, shared, layout, settings) and `src/views/`. Load when touching UI, workspace widgets, overlays, editors, or view-level wiring.

## Views

| Abstraction | Location | Purpose |
|-------------|----------|---------|
| ROUTE_WORKSPACE, ROUTE_SETTINGS, LEGACY_ROUTES, SettingsRouteState | routes.ts | Route paths for the hash router. `App.tsx` renders `WorkspacePage` at `/` (`ROUTE_WORKSPACE`, inside an `ErrorBoundary` scoped "Workspace"); `/settings` renders the mobile `SettingsPage` on a phone and, on desktop, opens the Settings modal and redirects to `/` (a deep link); every former page route in `LEGACY_ROUTES` (`/workspace`, `/list`, `/calendar`, `/dashboard`) and any unknown path redirects (`<Navigate replace>`) to `/`. Imported by `App.tsx`, `BottomTabBar` and `TopBar` |
| Section / buildListSections / listSortComparator | services/list-sections.ts | The section builder behind every list widget. See the `architecture/services-and-utils.md` row for the full surface |
| WorkspacePage, useWorkspaceData, useWorkspaceDragState, PanelGhost, EditorLeaveGuard | views/WorkspacePage.tsx | `/` — the app's main page. Renders `WorkspaceView` on desktop and `MobileWorkspaceView` on mobile (`useIsMobile`; its top bar gets the storage `SyncStatusButton`); on mobile the `DndContext`'s pointer sensor is swapped for `NoDragSensor` (no activators — the sensor list keeps its length, which dnd-kit uses as effect deps), so nothing drags. Calls `useWorkspaceStore.init()` on mount when not hydrated (pages mount after App's startup chain, so after the sync-file load). `useWorkspaceData` loads **all** tasks (`loadAll`), people, tags, statuses and list definitions, plus `useEntityAssignmentsForLoadedTodos()` — so chips and person / tag groups render on a cold load; the page subscribes to no task data, so a task edit doesn't re-render it (`src/test/views/workspace-page-renders.test.tsx` counts its renders). Owns the page `DndContext` (`buildTaskCollision`: tab and panel drags match no droppables, everything else `pointerWithin`): tab drags (`active.data.type === 'workspace-tab'`) and panel drags (`'workspace-panel'`, a whole card by its surface in the layout edit mode, E3) and a tray chip's new widget (`registerNewWidgetDrag`: source `{ type: 'new', kind }`, not dnd-kit's — its own window pointer-up drops it, Esc cancels it) — on drag start the page measures the workspace (`measureWorkspace`) — and again, once a frame, while anything scrolls under the drag (dnd-kit's auto-scroll at an edge; cr31 W3, `e2e/tab-drag-autoscroll.spec.ts`) —, a window `pointermove` listener runs `hitTestWorkspace` → `dropTargetForHit` → `dropGhost` (the probe: `null` for a drop that changes nothing, which isn't accepted) into `WorkspaceDragContext` (`WorkspaceDragState`: the source, the tabs that move, hit, target, accepts, the ghost, a strip's insert line), and drop calls `moveTab` / `movePanel` (a new widget: `widget-hooks.addWidgetKindAt`) — in the mode what moved is selected where it landed and a card keeps the focus (`edit-focus.focusEditItem`); Esc cancels. Task drags go through `dispatchTaskDrop` (calendar day; which date it sets: see `resolveCalendarDropType`; the write is `buildRescheduleUpdate` → `useTodoStore.update`) — its `chooseType` opens `CalendarDropChooser` (`useCalendarDropChoice`; the dropped-on day stays highlighted through `PendingCalendarDropContext`). Auto-scroll only scrolls a container while the pointer is inside it (`useAutoScrollUnderPointer`: dnd-kit otherwise scrolls the dragged row's list from another column). `DragOverlay` shows the tab ghost or, for a panel drag, `PanelGhost` (a 180px card: the panel's tabs, three placeholder lines, an `N tabs` badge, rotated −1.2°) — +12/+10 from the pointer via a modifier, placed above / left of the pointer where it would leave the window (where the drop lands is `WorkspaceView`'s one ghost, labelled), a lifted calendar entry (`calendarEntry` drag data) or the task row (`DragOverlayTask`, below). Only the task row's drop animates (`dropAnimation` is `createTaskDropAnimation().animation` while the last drag started on a task row — `liftedDrag` — else `null`: tab, panel and calendar-entry ghosts just go); the page tells it where the drop lands through `TaskDropLandingContext` (`in-place` for a drop on a droppable — a calendar day —, which the page sets, and for a list's accepted cross-group drop, which `ListWidget` sets; `settle` otherwise). Hosts `TaskDialog`, `CheckinDialog` (after them, so it opens over the task dialog), `CheckinImportDialog`, `TaskEditPopupHost`, `EntityActionHost`, `EditListHost`, `NumberEditorHost` and `ChartEditorHost` (D26: the Number and chart editors, outside the widget tree so no key or press in them reaches a cell). Nothing is ever unsaved (layouts and lists autosave), so it has no `beforeunload` prompt; `EditorLeaveGuard` blocks an in-app route change (`useBlocker`) only while an open editor holds changes, until it was saved or discarded (`stores/editor-leave`) |
| SettingsPage | views/SettingsPage.tsx | Mobile Settings (`/settings`, the bottom tab bar's Settings tab): the sections with a part flagged `mobile` in `settings-sections.ts` (`MOBILE_SETTINGS_SECTIONS`: Database, Appearance, People statuses & tags, About) as a list; tapping one slides to it — only its phone parts (People statuses & tags: only the Unused people & tags row; Database: Import / Export, File storage) — descriptions shown under their labels (`SettingsMobileContext`) — with a back arrow; navigated to with route state `{ section, part? }` (`SettingsRouteState` — the storage status sheet's File storage settings) it opens on that section, scrolled to the part (`scrollToSettingsPart`). Desktop shows the same sections in `SettingsModal` |

## Workspace Components

| Abstraction | Location | Purpose |
|-------------|----------|---------|
| WorkspaceView, EmptyLayout, Table, Dividers, DragOverlayLayer · useExpandOrphanRows | components/workspace/WorkspaceView.tsx · use-expand-orphan-rows.ts | `useExpandOrphanRows(layout)`: expands, as view state, the collapsed rows the open layout arrives with that nothing can expand (`workspace-grid.orphanCollapsedRows`) — a layout stored before the ops expanded them (a valid row is opened as stored), or an undo that carried a collapse onto an older table. Renders the working layout (D26): `[data-ws-area]` holds the grain, the ambience and the layout's table — one CSS grid `[data-ws-table]` (`data-ws-row-tracks`: its row track ids in order, for the drag's measure) whose `grid-template-*` come from `utils/workspace-table.trackTemplate` (a weighted track `minmax(<its cells' minimum>px, <share>fr)` — the table scrolls when the minimums don't fit —, a fixed track (a former band) its px, a fit row of Numbers `auto`, a collapsed row a header high), each cell placed with `grid-area` (collapsed when every row track it covers is, `isCellCollapsedIn`; at its small face in a fit row — a collapsed one too, not one a divider gave a height: `workspace-table.rowDrawsFitFaces`). `Dividers`: one `WorkspaceDivider` per stretch of grid line (`workspace-grid.edgeStretches`: a line parts at its "+" crossings, not at a "T"), a grid item in the gutter before the track after its line along its stretch, labelled `Resize <title> and <title>` (the first cells either side) — none beside a collapsed row; beside a Numbers-only row (`isFitTrack`) its pieces join back up and the divider gets `fitTrackIds` (it gives the row a height) —, and `partOfLine` when its stretch isn't the whole line (`isWholeLine`: Alt-drag breaks it off; its tooltip says so). No crossing handles (layout edit mode E4: a divider's plain drag moves its whole line). While a resize drag previews a structural move (`workspace-store.resizePreview`), `Table` draws the working copy as the preview (`utils/workspace-preview.previewedLayout`: its tracks, each cell — the same element, in the same order — at its area there), and the dragged handle keeps the key it had at the drag's start (a divider found again by `edgeBetween` its two cells), so it is never remounted under the pointer. No cells → the empty-layout block with kind tiles (`AddWidgetButtons` adding to `{kind: 'layout'}`; a List with a new list, no editor opening). While a tab or cell is dragged in the layout edit mode (outside it a tab only reorders in its own strip, with no ghost — E4), `DragOverlayLayer` (fixed, `aria-hidden`) draws the one drop ghost (D26 board T, `ws-drop-ghost`): `WorkspaceDragState.ghost` (`workspace-drop.dropGhost`) — the place and size the drop will produce, labelled (`ws-drop-label`: Split Late · above, Insert row · full width, Add as tab); it fades in at each new spot (reduced motion: at once) In the layout edit mode the area carries `data-layout-editing`: tinted (`--edit-canvas`) under a 16px dot grid (`--edit-dot`) with an inset accent outline (`--edit-outline`); each `[data-ws-cell-body]` dims (`--edit-cell-dim`) and takes no presses — the cell is a card, its surface and controls take them (`EditCard`, E3); nothing moves. The selected cell (`data-edit-selected`, `FilledCell` / `EmptyCell`) has an accent ring. |
| WorkspaceCell, WorkspaceCellProps, EmptyCell · FilledCell, StillRoom | components/workspace/WorkspaceCell.tsx (the empty cell and the switch) · FilledCell.tsx (a cell with tabs) | A cell of the table (`[data-ws-cell]`, `data-ws-cell-title` its active tab's title for the ghost's label): an empty cell (after a split, a close with no taker or a lone widget removed: `Add a widget` and the kind buttons, each added as its tab; outside the layout edit mode an **Edit layout** link — the mode, this cell selected; layout edit mode E4 —; in the mode a slot, `EditCard.EditSlot`, and a close ×, E3), else its active widget's body at its face. **Tabs on demand** (D26 AA1): with one widget no tab strip and no header — the widget's own top line (`WidgetTopLine`) names it and holds the controls slot the cell lends it through `CellControlsContext` (Open larger where the widget offers it — `CellFace.opensLarger` — and the ⋯); a face whose top line can't hold the slot (a Number's label is inside its button, a small face) gets the cell's own, floating over its top-right corner (`claim`: drawn only while no top line holds one). With two or more widgets the header: the tab strip, its empty space (`StillRoom`, only room for the strip to measure — in the mode a press there falls through to the card's surface; a click opens nothing, J15), Open larger (`open-larger`, where the active tab's widget offers it — `cellFace.opensLarger`; the strip observes the header's other parts, so it fits its tabs again when the button comes or goes; hidden by a container query on the header, `cell-header`, under 340px, where a long active tab beside `+N` needs the room), Collapse row and the ⋯. The ⋯'s widget part has **Open larger** before Name and icon… for the menu's tab (`tabOpensLarger` at the face it shows; a tab out of sight at its large face). Two menus, one state (`menu.part`, `cell-menu.CellMenuPart`; UX cleanup 4A, E3, E4): outside the mode either ⋯ opens the widget's (`widget`: the active widget's entries — what its tab menu had — then Add widget as tab ›, Remove widget — Add tab ›, Remove tab in a cell showing a tab strip —, Collapse row, Edit layout), under the ⋯; in the layout edit mode (E3) the cell is a card — `EditCardSurface` first in the cell (under the content, which takes no presses: `data-edit-layer`), `EditCardControls` last (its label, its ⋯ `Cell layout`, its corner), and every menu of the cell is the card's (`EditCardMenu`; the header's own Open larger, Collapse and ⋯ give way to it; a right-click anywhere on the card opens it at the point; a click on a strip's Number is a tap on the card); a right-click on a tab opens the whole menu (`all`, outside the mode the same as `widget`) for that tab; alone in its cell, a right-click on the top line (on a glance widget, anywhere not handled) and Shift+F10 / the Menu key in it (or on a control holding it, a Number's button) open the whole menu, at the pointer or under the ⋯. A second click on the ⋯ closes its menu. Collapsed with one widget, the cell still renders its widget (its large face) and shows only its top line: the rest is clipped and hidden (`visibility`), a glance widget takes its content's height, and the slot adds an Expand row button that always shows. While a drag joins it (its body's middle) a strip shows the ghost tabs — a one-widget cell's in the strip it will have (`CellTabStrip.JoinPreviewStrip`) over its top line; a strip's insert line marks a strip drop. **Faces** (D25 R, `cell-face.useCellFace`): the active widget reports its face through `WidgetFaceContext` (`[data-face]` on the cell); a fit row's cell forces `small`, a collapsed one-widget cell `large`. At the small face a click on the body opens the widget larger (the widget popup, `WidgetPopup`) unless the widget handled the click (a Number's drill, the slot's buttons: `preventDefault`) or doesn't open larger, and a focus-only `SmallFaceOpener` (`Open <title>`) is the keyboard's way. Closing a tab or cell or changing a tab's kind asks nothing (UX cleanup 3A; a list tab is never pointed at another list, 4A): a list its last tab leaves goes with the edit, and Undo brings it back. A tab's ×, Delete on a tab, a strip Number's × and Remove widget all run `workspace-store.removeWidget` (a lone widget's cell stays, empty — E4); Add widget as tab › runs `widget-hooks.addTabToCell` |
| NumberStrip, NumberSlot, NUMBER_SLOT_ATTR | components/workspace/NumberStrip.tsx, NumberStrip.module.css | A cell of only Numbers, two or more (flair P10, `isNumberStripCell`; `FilledCell` draws it in place of its body and tab strip): its Numbers side by side, evenly divided (`repeat(auto-fit, minmax(72px, 1fr))`, wrapping), each a slot (`[data-number-slot]`) with its own `useCellFace` / held height / `CellControlsContext` (its Open larger and ⋯ — the cell's menu state, opened at a point under the slot's ⋯ — on its own hover, `data-slot-hot`; no grip), its × (`Remove “X”`, as the tab strip's × and the ⋯'s Remove widget — `removeWidget`, the cell kept) and a tab's `useDraggable` (`workspaceTabDragId`: reordering in the strip; out of it, and into it from another card, only in the layout edit mode, where the slot itself takes presses and its insides none, E3); a `+` (Add number: `addWidget('number', cell)`) at the bottom of the strip's right-hand gutter (`padding-right`, `--strip-gutter`). `FilledCell` keeps its results and slot faces by tab, finds a right-click's Number by its slot, and draws the drop line between slots (`.stripInsertLine`). |
| insertPreview, splitPreview, showStructurePreview · GutterPlus · GutterHover, GutterPlusContext, useGutterPlus, GUTTER_PLUS_ATTR · EditCardToolbar | components/workspace/insert-preview.ts · GutterPlus.tsx (+ .module.css) · gutter-plus.ts · EditCardToolbar.tsx (+ .module.css) | Layout editor round two (concept A, 2026-11-19), in the layout edit mode. `insertPreview` / `splitPreview`: an insert on a run of lines or a card's split as a `StructurePreview` (`utils/structure-preview.structureProbe`; its `hint` in words — `Row between 1 and 2 · Notes stretches across it · click to insert`), which `WorkspaceView` draws (`data-preview-new` slots, `data-preview-grew` outlines with `stretches`, no dividers) and the mode bar's tray shows (`layout-edit-preview-hint`). A ruler "+" hovered stays fixed where the pointer found it while its preview reflows the table (`LayoutRulers`, `[data-pinned]`; leaving ends it, a click inserts). `GutterPlus`: a divider hovered in the mode shows a pill just beside its line, following the pointer along it (`[data-gutter-plus]`; the table lends the dividers its setter through `GutterPlusContext`, hiding it 250ms after the divider's leave); hovered it previews, clicked it inserts on the line (a sliver's run, `sliverRun`). `EditCardToolbar`: the selected card's toolbar (`role=toolbar`, `<name> card`, `[data-edit-toolbar]`), fixed above it (inside it under its header in the top row), frozen while a preview shows, none while dragging — Split → / ↓ (hover previews), Swap ▾, Merge ▾ (`mergeNeighbour` per side), Remove widget / tab. Every trigger clears its preview when it unmounts. |
| LayoutRulers, Rulers · useDrawnTracks, DrawnTracks | components/workspace/LayoutRulers.tsx · LayoutRulers.module.css · use-drawn-tracks.ts | The layout edit mode's rulers (E2), rendered first inside the table while the mode is on: grid items on the table's own tracks (so they align and scroll with it), overlaid on the gutters and the cells' outer 8px — the mode moves the workspace's top / bottom padding into the table's scroller, so no cell moves. A ruler per major column (its letter; its share in the tooltip) along the top and per major row (number + `fit` / `folded`) down the left, each a button (`Column B options`, `aria-haspopup="menu"`) opening a `MenuPopover`: Insert column left / right · Even all widths, or Insert row above / below · Collapse / Expand row · Even all heights, then Remove column / row with `removeTrackMessage` under it; a sliver's unlabelled tick (`Narrow column after A options`, a pill in the strip) offers Merge into A / B. A "+" (`Insert row here`, a 20px disc in a 24px target, its tooltip naming the major lines around it) on every line and at both ends — a sliver and its two lines are one line (`sliverRun`): one "+", just before the sliver (`data-sliver-run`), which merges it away and inserts on the line left, as the ruler's Insert above / below beside it do —, while hovered the table drawn as the insert would leave it (round two, concept A: `insert-preview.insertPreview` → `workspace-store.structurePreview`, the "+" itself pinned (fixed) meanwhile; the "+" itself quiet at rest, its accent disc on hover), inserting a full track and selecting its first new cell. Slivers read the table's computed tracks (`use-drawn-tracks.useDrawnTracks` over `divider-drag.drawnSizes`), measured after each layout change and on a resize — the table measures them too in the mode, for the cards' and slots' ruler names (`layout-edit-view`). |
| TabNameIconPopover · TabIcon | components/workspace/TabNameIconPopover.tsx (+ .module.css) · TabIcon.tsx | **Name and icon…** (items 11, 14; the cell's ⋯ / right-click widget part, `widget-kind-menu-entries.NAME_AND_ICON_LABEL`, just before Change widget ›; desktop only): a popover under the tab (or the top line standing for it) with NAME (its own name as stored — a list's, a Number's or a chart's, else the tab's; placeholder the default; a note for the kinds with a name of their own) and ICON (`IconPickerButton`: the icon shown and a summary; opens `IconPicker`, each pick `setTabIcon`). The name is written when the popover closes any way (Enter, Esc, a press outside — the popover rule), one step (`renameTab`). `TabIcon`: a tab's strip icon (`tabStripIcon`, `data-tab-icon`) wherever a tab is named — `CellTabStrip`'s tabs, ghost and join-preview tabs and `+N` menu, the drag overlay's ghosts (`DraggedTab.icon`), the widget popup's header; none for No icon. Alone in its cell `WidgetTopLine` leads with a picked icon (J2) and `WidgetNameLine` / the Review top line / a named Calendar read the tab's title |
| cellMenuFor, CellMenuPart, CELL_MENU_LABEL, addTabEntry, collapseEntry, mergedCellMenu, useWidgetMenuContent, CellMenuActions, NO_MERGE_REASON · editCardMenuEntries, EditCardMenuActions, EDIT_CARD_MENU_LABEL | components/workspace/cell-menu.tsx | The cell's menus (D26 AA1, UX cleanup 4A; layout edit mode E3, E4 — edit mode changes the grid; what's inside a cell changes in place): `cellMenuFor(widget, actions)` — outside the mode the ⋯ (`Cell options`) and a right-click / Shift+F10 (`CellMenuPart` `widget` / `all`, one menu): the widget's entries, then Move to layout › / Copy to layout › (`transfer`: `useTransferMenu('tab', id)` — the layouts, the open one off with `this layout`, then New layout…, which `ui-store.requestLayout({ kind: 'new', transfer })` hands to `LayoutControls`' name dialog; a pick runs `workspace-store.transferTo`; none while widgets are in hand), then `addTabEntry` (**Add widget as tab ›**: every kind under Tasks and Insights, a Breakdown's presets — `CellMenuActions.onAddTab(kind, preset?)`), Remove widget (the menu's tab — a lone widget's cell stays, empty; in a cell showing a tab strip, `showsTabStrip`, the two read Add tab › and Remove tab, a Numbers strip keeping the widget words), `collapseEntry` (Collapse / Expand row), then **Edit layout** (`onEditLayout`: the mode, this cell selected). `useWidgetMenuContent(tab, result)` — the widget part, what its tab menu had (a list's Edit list… / Review these N tasks / Zoom, Copy tasks, Collapse / Expand all groups; a Neglect map's settings; a glance widget's settings and Copy; then Change widget ›, a change to List making a blank list of the layout) as `WidgetKindMenuContent`; `mergedCellMenu(widget, cell)` — the widget's entries, one separator, the cell's (no separator doubled or at an end). `editCardMenuEntries(actions)` — a card's menu in the mode (**Cell layout**, `EditCard.EditCardMenu`): Move to ▸ (`slots`: every empty cell by its ruler name), Swap with ▸ (`cards`: every other card by its name — `layout-edit-cards.cardName`, all its tabs —, its ruler name as the note) and Join as tabs ▸ (the same cards, `onJoin`: this card's tabs join it), each off saying why when empty; Insert ▸ (`onInsertTrack`: Row above / Row below / Column left / Column right, a full track on the card's edge line; then Split this cell: `onSplit`, New cell above / left / right / below); Merge with ▸ Above / Left / Right / Below (off, saying why, unless a rectangle: `mergeWith`, `workspace-grid.mergeNeighbour`); Stretch right / Stretch down (`canStretch`: an empty slot all along that side); Move cell to layout › / Copy cell to layout › (`transfer`, `useTransferMenu('cell', id)`); then Remove widget and Close cell (`(N tabs)` with two or more). |
| EditCardSurface, EditCardControls, EditCardCorner, EditCardMenu, EditSlot · CardMenuAt, ARROW_SIDES, selectOnFocus, focusAfterTap, moveSelection, onCardKey, tapCard, drawnAxis, stretchLabel · EDIT_FOCUS_ATTR, focusEditItem · LayoutEditViewContext, LayoutEditView, StretchPreview, useLayoutEditView, useRulerNames | components/workspace/EditCard.tsx, EditCard.module.css · edit-card-actions.ts · edit-focus.ts · layout-edit-view.ts | The layout edit mode's cards and slots (E3). `EditCardSurface` (`data-edit-surface`, `data-edit-focus`, a Tab stop, `role="button"`, its name `Notes card, B2. … Delete removes Notes`): under the card's content, a dnd-kit panel drag (`ws-panel:<id>:card`) from anywhere on the card, a click a tap (`workspace-store.tapEdit`, its zone by `workspace-drop.bodyZone`), its focus selecting it (not while a tap armed a move), its keys (`onCardKey`: arrows → `spatialNeighbour`, Shift+arrow → `stretchCell`, Alt+arrow → `altMove` (`moveEditCard` onto a slot / `swapCells`), Enter / Shift+F10 / the Menu key its menu, Space a tap, Delete / Backspace `removeWidget` on its active tab). `EditCardControls`: its label (icon and name; `N Numbers` on a strip; none over a tab strip; takes no presses) and, for a tab strip, `stripLabel` — the active tab's name under the strip, drawn only while the strip is squeezed to that tab with its title cut away (`CellTabStrip`'s `data-strip-squeezed`, a narrow card), its ⋯ (`Cell layout`), its corner (`EditCardCorner`: a pointer drag over the table's drawn tracks — `drawnAxis`, `trackReached`, `cornerArea` — previewed through `LayoutEditViewContext.setStretch`, Esc cancels, the release `setCellArea`). `EditCardMenu`: mounted only while open (it reads the whole layout), `editCardMenuEntries` over the draft, the focus restored where it was. `EditSlot`: an empty cell's button (`Empty slot B4`, dashed), a click a tap (`menu` → `AddWidgetFlow`; with a card armed, the move), the arrows, Delete closing it. `focusEditItem(cellId)` puts the focus on a card or slot after the move that remounted it (next frame). `layout-edit-view`: what the table lends them in the mode — the rulers' names of the drawn tracks (`trackNames`) and the stretch preview's setter (the table draws `ws-stretch-ghost` on the area, `Stretch · A2–B2`). |
| LayoutEditTray, TrayChip, MoveHint | components/workspace/LayoutEditTray.tsx, LayoutEditTray.module.css | The layout edit mode's widget tray (E3), in the mode bar's tray slot: the common kinds as chips (List, Calendar, Notes, Number, Breakdown; `data-tray-kind`) and **More ›** (a `MenuPopover` of the rest). A chip dragged past 5px starts a new widget's drag (`workspace-drag.beginNewWidgetDrag`, its pointer capture released so the page's drag follows the pointer); a click adds the kind to the selected slot or card (`widget-hooks.addWidgetKindAt`; with none, a notice). Scrolls sideways (no bar) when the bar is narrow. While a tap armed a move, a live region's hint takes the chips' place (`Click a slot to move “X” there, a card to swap, or its tab bar to join it · Esc cancels`, X the card's name). |
| WidgetTopLine, WidgetNameLine, TopLineName, CellControls · CellControlsContext, CellControlsValue, TOP_LINE_ATTR | components/workspace/WidgetTopLine.tsx, WidgetTopLine.module.css · cell-controls.ts | A widget's top line (D26 AA1, `[data-widget-top-line]`): its own first line — a list's filter line (or, its filter line hidden, `ListNameLine`), the calendar's header, a Number's label (`controls="cell"`), a section's panel header, a small face's title — which, alone in its cell (`CellControlsContext` set), starts with its `lead` (the widget's name), stands for the tab (`data-ws-tab`) and ends with the cell's controls slot. `alone`: drawn only alone in its cell (`WidgetNameLine`: Notes; the Neglect map with Slipping N). `CellControls`: an Expand row button while collapsed (always shown), then Open larger (where the widget offers it) and the ⋯ (`Cell options`, the widget's menu) — shown on the cell's `:hover` and `:focus-within`, while a menu of the cell is open (`data-open`) and always on touch (`data-touch`), opacity only and with no fade (in a top line the slot is a flex item of its own: nothing lies under it); `floating`, its box always and its hidden controls take no press (`pointer-events: none`), so a press or tap at that spot reaches the face under it — before UX cleanup 4A the hidden floating ⋯ took the presses on a Number's corner; `floating`: the cell's own over its corner, which stands for the tab (`data-ws-tab`, a top line's right-click and Shift+F10) while the face has no top line at all (the calendar's and the Neglect map's small faces). In the layout edit mode the slot isn't drawn (the card's ⋯ stands for it, E3). Elsewhere (a strip names the widget, the small face's popover, Settings) the top line is just the line |
| MobileWorkspaceView, MobileTopBar, LayoutSheet, ListTabSheet, MobileTable, MobileCell | components/workspace/MobileWorkspaceView.tsx | The workspace on a phone (D26): the top bar (layout picker → the layout sheet, the storage status, the `61 of 109` pill, search), then every cell of the table in reading order (`[data-ws-scroll]` › `[data-ws-table]`, by its top track then its left) in one vertical scroll; a run of Number cells sits two up while each gets 140px. A cell follows the desktop's tabs on demand (D26 AA1): one widget, no tab row — its top line holds the ⋯ (always shown, `touch`: no grip), and a face whose top line can't hold it has none; two or more, the touch tab strip (a glance cell's 30px). Its ⋯: the active widget's phone items (a list's Edit list… and Copy tasks), then Collapse / Expand row; a list tab's long-press its list sheet. Collapsed with one widget, its top line shows (the rest hidden, at most the touch filter line high), with Expand row in its controls slot — where no top line holds the slot (a Number's label is inside its button), the cell's own Expand row button over its right end (it counts the slot's claims only while collapsed). No layout editing |
| CellTabStrip, TabBadgeCapsule | components/workspace/CellTabStrip.tsx | A list tab's count shows on the tab only while its list's filter line is hidden (the line carries it otherwise) and the screen filter narrows the list (UX cleanup 2A: one number per list — else none). A list tab's badge (`useTabBadges`, `[data-tab-badge]`; drawn by `shared/BadgeCapsule`, as the top bar's counter's): the due and follow-up counts in one capsule after the title, solid per part while some of it is overdue, its words in the tab's `title`, also in the `+N` menu (`MenuItemEntry.trailing`); a badge counts as chrome in the fit, and a changed count measures again; a badged tab's cap (160 px, `TAB_MAX_PX`) grows by its capsule and the gap before it, so its title gets the room it would have without one (cr31 M4: tabs grow to fit, the badge only adds). Tabs (`role=tab`, `[data-ws-tab]`, dnd-kit `useDraggable` with `WorkspaceTabDragData`): kind icon, title (`useTabTitle` — a list tab shows its list's name; the native tooltip is the title), the active list tab's count label (`shown of total`, only while the screen filter hides tasks), a 6px accent dot (`aria-label="unsaved list changes"`) when the tab's list (`tabListId`) has unsaved edits, close × on hover / active — on a device that hovers (`@media (hover: hover)`, not the phone's `.touch` strip) the active tab's × and the add-tab `+` show only on the cell's hover or focus-within (opacity, so the fit never changes; no fade — mid-fade a control is a stacking context over the tab beside it and took a press meant to drag the tab; UX cleanup 1A) — its place kept while hidden (`visibility`), so no tab changes width on hover (cr31 M4). ←/→/Home/End move + activate (the focus follows once the tab shows — a folded one is out of sight, so not focusable, until its activation renders, which a view transition holds back, and an editor's leave question past its answer; cr31 W6, cr34 X — a pick from the `+N` menu likewise: the picked tab takes the focus), Delete closes, Shift+F10 / ContextMenu opens the tab menu. Close ×, add `+` and the tab menu render only when their handlers are passed; `touch` (mobile) makes tabs taller and not draggable (no dnd-kit attributes) and leave `touch-action` at `auto`, so a swipe that starts on a tab still scrolls the page. A `ResizeObserver` measures the room the header gives the strip (its width less the other header parts; the panel handle — `data-strip-room` — counted at its 16px minimum) and each tab's full and least width (its parts plus the whole title / 48px of it; the active tab never shrinks), and `utils/tab-strip-fit.fitTabs` decides: every tab at full width while they fit, else every tab shrunk (equal shares, titles cut short), else as many shrunk tabs as fit beside the `+N ▾` overflow menu (named by `useTabTitles`, as the tabs are — a list's unsaved name included). Folded tabs stay mounted out of sight (`data-ws-tab-folded`, measured, left out of the drop geometry); a folded active tab is shown in the last visible place (display order only); when not even the active tab fits beside `+N` it shows alone, cut down like the others (`TabFit.squeeze`, its `least`) — never no tab. The first fit is measured in a layout effect, before the first paint; a `MutationObserver` on the tabs (child list and text, not attributes) measures again when a tab's own content changes — a shrunk tab's width is pinned, so no box resizes for a count, the unsaved dot or a rename (cr31 W5). Add-tab `+` |

No view state; config through its ⋯ menu (`neglect-menu`). **Faces** (D25 R; measured on a `.faceHost` the body's size, the full map may grow past it): small — `Slipping`, its count (deadline-coloured while > 0) of the open tasks placed, the threshold; no 7-day change (a task's untouched days a week ago aren't kept: its older `modifiedAt` is gone once it is touched, so a past Slipping count can't be rebuilt honestly); medium — the numbered Slipping list alone (`SlippingList`, shared with the full map), no plot, no selection **Names** (`[data-neglect-label]`, `aria-hidden` — a dot's own name says it): the full map measures its plot field and its strip field (`useElementSize`) and each title (`task/text-width.appTextWidth`, 11px medium) and draws the names `placeNeglectLabels` places — the Slipping tasks first in their list's order, then the flagged ones — at the dot's spot pushed off by `--label-offset`, on one 13px line cut with an ellipsis at its given width, `pointer-events: none` (a drag over a name still lassoes). **Zone words** (`ZoneWord`, `[data-neglect-zone]`): each corner's word and the strip's two labels show their count and, while it isn't 0, are buttons (`Slipping: show 4 tasks`) that open the zone's tasks in the task dialog (`neglectZoneDrill`: Slipping in its list's order, the others soonest due first then longest untouched; `noDue` every task without a due date); a press on one starts no lasso. **The Slipping list** shows beside the plot from `NEGLECT_SIDE_LIST_MIN_PX` (900px of widget; `data-slipping-list`), under it on a phone, and not at all on a narrower widget, whose plot then fills it (`data-layout` is `beside` everywhere but a phone) and whose dots have no badges. |

From the drag preview (`planner-store.drag`, this tab's): `data-planner-drop` (`followup` / `do`) on the target day's tile and group — dashed in that date's color, the tile tinted — when the drop writes (`PlannerDropMark`); the list's `DropSlot` in the hovered group at the preview's place, counted among the rows without the dragged one (none where the row already is; it takes the place of Today's empty line); each row's look the list's (`plannerRowLook`: the dragged row `slot` in its own group or over no target, `gone` once its slot shows elsewhere, `origin` when headed for another day; the others `dim` while they make room); a refused drop the not-allowed cursor (`useDropRefusedCursor`). On dnd-kit's `onDragOver` / `onDragMove` it takes `over` when it is one of this tab's droppables (`isPlannerDropData`, `scope`), so a clipped or covered target never shows; on the window's `pointermove` and a scroller's scroll it recomputes with the pointer's own position: `plannerDrop` for the task's placement in the droppable's window, and over a group the place — rows above the pointer among the group's rows without the task's own (`listRowDropTarget`) — only where the drop brings the row into the group or it is there already. Off its targets, at the drag's end or cancel, on a release dnd-kit never reports (`watchDragRelease`) and on unmount it clears its own preview, never another Planner's. `src/test/components/workspace/planner-drag-hook.test.tsx` |
| PlannerDragLabel | components/workspace/planner/PlannerDragLabel.tsx | The lifted row's label over a Planner (`planner-drag-label`, in `DragOverlayTask`'s `label` slot and beside a calendar entry's ghost): `plannerDropLabel` of the store's drag preview — `Follow-up · Wed, Nov 25` on the solid follow-up color, `Do · Wed, Nov 25` / `Do · Today` / `No day` on the Focus color (`data-tone`), 11px bold, 5px under the row; none for a refused drop, one that writes nothing, or no Planner target. `data-above` (set from the row's box as the pointer moves — the overlay moves without a render) puts it above the row where it would leave the window at the bottom. |
| plannerMoveEntries, MOVE_FOLLOWUP_LABEL, MOVE_DO_LABEL, NO_DAY_REFUSED | components/workspace/planner/planner-menu.tsx | **Move to day ›** for a Planner row's task menu (`TaskMenuExtrasContext`; none for a completed task): the item (the Planner glyph, the row's day as its note) with a submenu — a `label` line (`Moves the follow-up` for a row on its day by its follow-up, else `Sets the Do day`), Today (its date as a note) and the window's days after it in words as radio items, the row's day checked, a separator, **No day** (checked for a Focus task with no day; clears a Do day; `disabled` with `title` = why — `NO_DAY_REFUSED` — for a row placed by a date). A pick calls `onMove(target)`: the widget's is `services/planner-drop.movePlannerTask`. `src/test/components/workspace/planner-menu.test.tsx` |
| useListRowDrag | components/workspace/list/use-list-row-drag.ts | `useDndMonitor` for this widget's row drags (drag data `listTabId`): measures the widget when the drag starts (`measureListGeometry` — so rows making room don't move the targets), follows window `pointermove` to the landing spot (`listRowDropTarget`; outside the scroll area → none) — the listener is attached in `onDragStart` itself, so the move that starts the drag already counts — and calls `onDrop` on release. The landing spot follows the pointer's moves and the list's scroll under a still pointer (its auto-scroll at an edge; cr31 W7, `e2e/list-drag-autoscroll.spec.ts`) A drag dnd-kit ends without a monitor event (a quick flick, released before the drag's first render) ends on the release too, dropping nothing (`watchDragRelease`, `e2e/list-drag-flick.spec.ts`) |
| watchDragRelease | components/workspace/drag-release.ts | dnd-kit 6 calls neither `DndContext`'s props nor its monitors when a drag ends before the render its start scheduled has committed (its end event is built from the sensor context a layout effect fills). Watches the pointer sensor's ends — the release, a pointer cancel, Esc, a resize, the page hidden — and, the next task (after dnd-kit's own synchronous end), calls `orphaned` while `isCurrent` says the drag is still on. Used by `useListRowDrag` and the workspace page's tab / card drag state |

Typeahead filters by label as the user types; a pick while the input has text clears it (the focus stays, every option shows again — the picks are pills above, so the list never re-sorts). The dropdown portals to `document.body` through `usePopoverAnchor` (ref anchor, reposition mode: `closeOnScroll` / `closeOnResize` false; Escape owned by the input), so it flips above / clamps inside the viewport and escapes the widget's `overflow` clipping `inputRef` (optional): its field, for the list's empty state to focus. The form is `shared/ListEditorBody` over Edit list's draft (`useEditListModel`: `list-draft-store.edit` — every widget showing the list draws it live; an edit back to the saved value drops the draft; in memory only, dropped however the surface closes) with `saved` = the saved list (changed rows marked). **Save** writes what the draft changed since it opened (`configPatch` against the list as it opened, applied over the list as it is then) through `list-definition-store.editList` — for good, one undo step `Edited list “<name>”` — and closes once the write has landed: one that fails (`Couldn't save list “<name>”.`), or that a data replace holds, leaves it open with its draft, to save again — its save returns whether the write landed, so a Save answered in a leave question raised from outside (Settings, a layout switch, another editor) that fails cancels that leave (`useEditorLeave`) — (while a Save is being written the draft counts as saved: nothing asks about it); off while nothing changed, the name is blank (`Name the list to save it`) or a filter expression doesn't read (`ExpressionReportProvider`, `SAVE_UNREADABLE_TITLE`). **Discard** drops the draft and closes. `requestClose`: no changes → closes; changes → `askChoice` Save / Discard / Keep editing (`Save changes to list “<name>”?`), the widget editors' question; it is registered with `edit-list-keep.setEditListCloseRequest` so the filter line's words and summary and `widget-hooks.openEditList` (another list's Edit list) ask through it (`requestEditListClose`). Its surface is the level-1 popover (`PopoverRoot`, D21 — the arrow in the popover's colour, ringed with `--color-popover-ring`), so every picker and menu opened from it is level 2. Header `EDIT LIST` + × (`Close Edit list`); the body; the hint `Changes show in the list right away…`; footer `Shared · N widgets` / `In 1 widget`, Discard and Save (the widget editors' foot). Focus starts in Name and goes back to the opener (else the list's tab) on close. The user asks it to close (`requestClose`) — Esc from inside it or with nothing focused (bubble phase, so a menu or picker inside, or the close question, takes its own Esc first), ×, a press in the app outside it (the list's own panel included; not portaled layers — menus, pickers, dialogs, the question — nor the controls marked `data-edit-list-keep` with its list's id: the filter line's words and summary, `edit-list-keep.ts`), the summary or words toggle, another list's Edit list (`widget-hooks.openEditList`): with no changes it closes at once and a press goes on to what it pressed; with changes it asks, and the press that asked goes no further (`usePressElsewhereCloses`) — but a strip tab's, made again once the answer is Save or Discard (`carryOnAfterLeave`). Settings opening, another editor opening, a layout switch, a route change, and whatever in a cell or the widget popup would take the list off the screen (a tab switched or closed, a collapse, a merge, Change widget, the popup closed) ask first (`stores/editor-leave`, through `hooks/use-editor-leave`); the list leaving the screen where nothing could ask (an undo, a cell resized to its small face) or being deleted, a data replace and the workspace unmounting close it at once, and with changes it says they weren't saved. Its filter pickers keep their Esc (`PredicateChips` `inDialog`): an Esc closes the picker, not Edit list. **Phone** (`EditListSheet`): a bottom half sheet (55dvh, `variant="sheet"` form, Discard · Save) with a light scrim over the list above (a tap asks to close); the handle drags it up to full height or down to close (the scrim, the handle, × and Esc ask as the popover's closes do); focus on the sheet, not Name No caret: its own arrow at the panel's tab strip, ringed in `--color-popover-ring` with the dark theme's rim (`--color-popover-rim`) outside, as the carets are. |

Clicks / pointer events / right-clicks inside don't bubble to the opener. The panel carries `data-shortcut-scope="none"`, so ↑↓ / Enter / Space in a menu never reach the app shortcuts behind it. Surface (D21): the popover tokens on a `PopoverRoot` — level 1 over the page, 2 in a dialog or opened from another popover; its submenus and panels are a level above the menu (it wraps them in its level); a dragged item lifts on the level-2 surface and shadow A caret (D21) by default when anchored to an element (`anchorRef`, or a `point` with its element's `rect`, e.g. + Filter's menu) and placed below or above it; none at a bare point (a context menu, the task menu at the pointer), beside the anchor (`right-start` / `left-start`), or on its submenus and panels; `caret` overrides.. An item that closes the menu runs with the focus already back — on what had it (`restoreFocus`), else on the trigger — so a dialog it opens returns the focus there, not to the item that is gone (cr31 T16) |

## Task Components

| Abstraction | Location | Purpose |
|-------------|----------|---------|
| TaskEditPopupHost | components/task/TaskEditPopupHost.tsx | The task editor for the workspace: the edit popup for `ui-store`'s selected task and the create popup (`openCreatePopup` — QuickAdd's Details handoff), wired through `useTaskEditCallbacks`. Closed, it subscribes to the editor's open state alone (the editor part mounts with it), so a hover or a task write never renders it. While the task dialog is open the edit popup is `nested` (stacked over it, the dialog still in view) and takes focus on open, so Esc closes only the editor. Mounted by `WorkspacePage` Create mode: Esc or the backdrop asks first when the form differs from how it opened (a title handed over from Quick Add counts; `Create task` / `Save draft`, Discard, Keep editing, or Discard / Keep editing with no title); its Cancel button discards without asking. A subtask typed and not added goes with Create. |
| TaskEditPopup, editor-fields | components/task/TaskEditPopup.tsx, components/task/editor-fields.ts | Centered modal for editing/creating tasks, 920px wide (`min(920px, 100vw − 32px)`) and **as tall as its content** (the taller column sets it; up to 82vh, then the columns scroll inside), in **the one task layout** it shares with the review card (task layout B, feedback 2026-11-15, board B-Editor): `TaskEditHeader` (box, title, edit mode's muted `Created … · Modified …` — `meta` —, ⋯ — Duplicate · Make subtask of… · Delete, each after the notes typed so far — and ×), then `TaskDetailsBody` — the fact band (`PriorityFacts`, `StatusFacts`, `TaskEditMetadata`'s People · Tags · Due) over the writing (`SubtasksSection`, then `TaskNotesSection`) beside Status updates (`StatusUpdatesSection`) — and `TaskEditFooter` (`followup`: `FollowupEditorGroup`'s Last and Next fields first; then edit mode's Mark followed up — `handleFollowedUp`, bulk-aware through `onFollowedUp` — and Done; create mode's Cancel / Create task, no Mark followed up: a new task's Last field takes a date). Ticking a task complete in its header closes it (reopening one leaves it open). **Edit mode and concurrent changes:** `onUpdate(changes)` gets only the fields the user changed (status / dates / repeat save as they change; the title on blur). The notes are their own edit session (`useTaskNotesSource`, written straight through `todo-store.update`): every other write to the task writes the notes typed so far first. The editor keeps a baseline — the task's values at its last sync with the store (`editorFieldsOf`); a store change (bulk edit, calendar drop, undo) shows in every field the user hasn't changed since (`storeUpdatesToShow`), a field being typed keeps the user's text, and saving sends only what differs from the baseline (`editorChanges`), so nothing changed elsewhere is written back. **Status updates** (the right-hand column): `status-updates/StatusUpdatesSection` — in edit mode each add / edit / delete writes at once through `status-update-store` (its own undo step, after the notes typed so far — `flushNotes`), not through the baseline diff (`editor-fields` has no such field); in create mode the entries are pending (local keys; the section shows them oldest first) and go to `onCreate` as `statusUpdates` (`EditorTaskFields`), written with the task (a seed's `TaskDraft.statusUpdates` start the list). Hosts people / **tags** chip selectors driven by `ChipSelector` (the person picker — `ChipSelector` with `avatars`, placeholder "Search or create people…", Create "<name>" via `entityCreators.onCreatePerson` = `person-store.getOrCreate`; person chips = an 18px avatar filled with `person.color` + the name in `text-secondary` + ×; Esc in a picker closes only the picker): tag props are `{assignedTags, allTags, onAssignTag, onUnassignTag, onCreateTag}`; create mode buffers picks into `pendingTagIds` and returns them through `onCreate(todo, {personIds, tagIds})` so the caller can route tag assignments through `tag-store.assignTag` after insert. `TaskRow` also exposes its tags cell (the shared `TagPicker`; the phone row's task menu opens the same picker); the popup is the full detail surface. First in the band, `PriorityFacts` (shared with the review card): the **Focus / Urgent / Important** marker toggles (label only, `aria-pressed`; off a hairline, on = `accent-bg-medium` + `marker-focus` / `danger-bg` + `marker-urgent` / `followup-bg` + `marker-important`); then `StatusFacts`, the status pill (`Status: <name>`). Urgent is the flag alone: pressed = flagged (`data-urgent="flag"`); when only the due date (the editor's current one) makes the task urgent it is unpressed with a faint clock after the label (`data-urgent="date"`, `data-urgent-hint`; the toggle always keeps the clock's room — its face centred over a hidden sizer, `markerSizer` — so the clock coming with a due date moves nothing, E6) and the tooltip `Mark urgent` + `Urgent by due date — due Tue 13. Click to flag it urgent too.`; flagged and due soon, the tooltip says the date keeps it urgent even unflagged (`utils/urgency.urgentToggleHint`). A click flips the flag, nothing asked. Edit mode applies at once (`bulkSetUrgent` / `bulkSetImportant` / `bulkSetFocus`, one undo step each); create mode keeps drafts (seeded by a Markers screen filter / list seed) passed to `onCreate` as `urgent` / `important` / `focus: true`. **Seeded / draft / nested (notes → tasks):** create mode takes `seed` (a `TaskDraft`: title with people / tags still to create appended as `@` / `#` tokens, fields, picks; no screen-filter defaults on top, no Quick Add draft read), `onSaveDraft` (draft mode: Save draft hands `(partial, assignments)` back instead of creating; no Last follow-up field) and `nested` (backdrop at `--z-dialog-overlay`, no blur, so it sits over the preview or another editor). Esc on a field leaves it and moves focus to the editor (a second Esc closes it); Esc never propagates past the editor, so a stacked one never closes the one below. A popup opened from a convert has notes without Convert Create mode: Create task / ⏎ / the close check read the subtasks and status updates through `committed()`, so a subtask or an update still typed in its line goes with the task. `onCreate` resolves the new id or `null`; on `null` (not stored — the snackbar says so) the editor stays open with every field as typed, so Create task can be pressed again (a second press while one runs is ignored) On the shared `DialogFrame` (`Edit task` / `New task`; overlay tier, `raised` when `nested`; 12vh from the top, full screen on a phone): focus in (the title in create mode, the panel in edit mode), Tab kept inside, a backdrop press, no app shortcuts inside, focus back on close; the first Esc in a text field only leaves it, the next closes — with nothing focused, Esc closes the editor (the topmost dialog), not what is under it |

Everything renders in a body portal. **With `subtaskTarget`** (a task's notes) none of this opens: Alt+T, the hover icon and the Convert scope write at once — what was typed first (its own edit), then the target (`noteSubtaskDrafts`, `addSubtasksFromNote`; create mode: the editor's pending subtasks), whose `writeNote` turns the lines `✓` (or removes them, with `removeConvertedLines`) inside the same undo group. `noteNameOf` = the note's first line when it's a heading (the preview's `From the note "…"`). **Subtasks** (cr28): a bullet's indented list lines (`note-lines.subtaskLinesUnder`) become its draft's `subtasks` (plain titles, a done box done) — shown under the preview row — and go with the line: Remove removes them too, Keep marks the bullet `✓` and removes them (their text now lives in the task); Alt+T on a bullet seeds them into the editor's Subtasks section |
