Keys (the frame's `onKeyDown`): ↑/↓ move, ⏎ edits, Del removes, Ctrl/⌘+⏎ creates, Esc cancels; `stopKeys` — no key reaches the notes surface it opened from (the task editor's own keys) |
| convertHighlight, setConvertHighlight, showConvertScope | components/task/notes/notes-convert-highlight.ts | CodeMirror line decorations (`cm-convert-scope`: accent-bg-subtle fill + 2px accent bar) for the lines a Convert would take, set while the button is hovered / focused; mapped through edits |
| notesNlp, notesPopupField, pickNotesPopupRow, highlightNotesPopupRow, holdNotesPopup, notesPopupFocusEffect, NotesNlpConfig | components/task/notes/notes-nlp.ts | The notes `@ # ! /` autocomplete as a CodeMirror extension; each line is task text of its own. `notesPopupField` holds `{ view, selectedIndex, lineFrom }` from `computeAutocomplete(caret line, offset)` — it opens only on typing (`input.type` / `delete`), re-evaluates on caret moves while open, and closes on any other edit (paste, toolbar, undo, external value), on blur, or on Esc. A row's person / tag menu holds it open (`holdNotesPopup(true)`, a `StateField`) while the focus is in the menu or the editor / confirm it opened; the editor getting the focus back (`notesPopupFocusEffect`), `holdNotesPopup(false)` or the popup closing ends the hold. Keys while open (↑/↓ no wrap, ⏎ / Tab pick — never a newline or tab, Esc) are taken in a window capture listener scoped to this editor, ahead of CodeMirror and every host's Escape / Enter handlers. `pickNotesPopupRow` replaces just the token (`applyAutocompletePick`), reopens on an argument command, and calls `onCreate` for a create row (`notes-nlp-stores` → `services/nlp-create`). `highlightNotesPopupRow` — the pointer moved over a row. Typing on past an `@` / `#` token (a typed space / punctuation, a caret move off it) completes it to the highlighted person / tag in the same step (`implicitAccept`, an `EditorState.transactionFilter`); a highlighted create row leaves the text as typed — a typed space never creates. Token tints: per visible line `parseInput` → `resolveInput` → `tokenTints` as `Decoration.mark` (`cm-nlp-tint` + `data-tint`; Quick Add's colours, radius 3, no padding); re-tinted when the config's `subscribe` reports a store change (a created person stops reading as new) |
| storeNotesNlpConfig | components/task/notes/notes-nlp-stores.ts | `NotesNlpConfig` over the live stores: candidates from `personItems` / `tagItems` / `statusItems` (memoized per array), `commands: true`, the new-task follow-up interval (`settings-store.autoFollowupInterval`, `null` while the automatic next follow-up is off); a create row calls `person-store.getOrCreate` (`@`) or `tag-store.add` (`#`, lookup-or-create) at once — a failure rejects with the user-facing message `Couldn't create …` that `NotesEditor` hands to `onNlpError`, so the entity exists before the line is converted; `subscribe` fires on people / tags / statuses array changes |
| NotesNlpPopup | components/task/notes/NotesNlpPopup.tsx | `NlpPopup` portalled to the body (`--z-dialog-nested`: above the task editor and note popovers it opens from, by DOM order; a row's person / tag menu and the editor / confirm it opens come later, so they cover it) at the trigger character's box (`coordsAtPos`) via `usePopoverAnchor` with a `rect` anchor (below, flips above, clamped; `contentKey` re-measures as rows change). A frame loop follows the caret through scrolls and layout shifts and closes the popup once the line scrolls out of the editor. Rows pick on mousedown (focus stays in the editor). `TaskNotePopover` treats clicks inside `[data-nlp-popup]` as inside. Person / tag rows have their menu (`entityMenu`: Shift+F10 in `contentDOM`; the hold above while it is open — released on a frame after it closes if the editor has the focus, else when the focus comes back; Show tasks closes the popup) |

## Overlays

| Abstraction | Location | Purpose |
|-------------|----------|---------|
| CommandPalette | components/overlays/CommandPalette.tsx | Ctrl+K palette (desktop; mounted by `App.tsx`'s `AppCommandPalette` over `command-registry.createCommands` while it shows, so the shell subscribes to no task, person or selection; the list is rebuilt from the tasks, people, assignments, statuses, check-in filter, layouts and theme as they change — a palette opened during the app's load gains the per-person check-ins as the data arrives —, and a command reads the stores when it runs). **Second steps** (3B): a command with a `step` (`Go to Layout ›` — a `›` at its row's end, `aria-haspopup`) opens its leaves in the same palette — a breadcrumb before the field (`Go to Layout ›`, `data-testid="command-palette-crumb"`, the field's `aria-describedby`), the field emptied (placeholder `Type to filter…`) and filtering the step (Fuse over the leaves' names), a step's `group`s as headings (Add Widget ›'s Tasks / Insights), the one in use marked `Current` (`current`); Backspace in the empty field and Esc each go back one step, the query and the step row as they were (Esc then closes); the footer then reads `Backspace or Esc Back`. The field keeps the focus while stepping (a row's press doesn't take it); it goes back to what had it only when a leaf runs or the palette closes. At rest the top level lists the step commands, not their leaves; **a query reaches into the steps**: each leaf is listed by its path (`Go to Layout › Planning`, `Review With › Aisha Rahman`), found when each word matches its own name or its step's — each counting where it matches better — and at least one its own (`aisha` finds her Review With leaf, `review aisha` too, `layout` or `go to lay` the step, not every leaf), scored and highlighted on the path. Commands are fuzzy-matched with Fuse over name + category: a one-word query as typed; with several words each word must match on its own (any order) and a command ranks by the sum of its one-word scores, so `clear filters` finds `Clear All Filters`. Matched letters are highlighted (every word's). Unless the query starts with `>` (commands only), `searchDynamicCommands` adds up to 20 task-title hits (`Search Results`). Results are grouped by category (Navigation, Layouts, Tasks, Bulk Actions, Filters, Search Results); a command's `hint` shows on the right; ↑↓ / Enter / Esc. On the shared `DialogFrame` (overlay tier, top-anchored at 10vh): Esc from anywhere in it, a backdrop press, Tab kept inside, focus back to where it was. Running a command puts the focus back on what had it before the palette first, so a dialog it opens at once (a task result → the editor) returns the focus there on close (cr31 N5) |
| BulkConfirmDialog | components/overlays/BulkConfirmDialog.tsx | Confirmation dialog for destructive bulk actions (delete, complete/uncomplete); supports custom messages, labels and a custom confirm handler. Built on the shared `ConfirmDialog` (`shared/Dialog`) — focus trap, Esc dismissal, autoFocus on the primary action are inherited. Rendered `nested` so backdrop + dialog land at `--z-dialog-nested` (1003) — keeps the singleton confirm above any other open dialog at `--z-dialog-overlay` (1002) |
| FollowupPopoverHost | components/overlays/FollowupPopoverHost.tsx | Shows the `FollowupPopover` a task row asks for (`ui-store.followupPopover`, `useTaskRowPickers`), outside the row: the popover stays open across a write that can take the row out of its list or group. Anchored on the `rect` the trigger had when it opened (it stays put while the row moves or goes), `triggerRef` the toggle, the level the row's place gives it (2 in a dialog); acts through `useBulkActions`, so on the selection a row is part of. Closes when its task is deleted or the layout switches. On a row in a multi-selection it starts from the selection (`utils/followup.sharedNextFollowup`, as the bulk bar's: their shared next follow-up or mixed), so a date equal to the clicked task's still goes to the others (cr31 T11) |
| RowPopoverHost | components/overlays/RowPopoverHost.tsx | Shows the notes popover (`TaskNotePopover`) or the checklist (`SubtasksPopover`, or the phone's `SubtasksSheet`) a task row asks for (`ui-store.rowPopover`, opened by `useTaskRowPickers`; a row's is open while its own trigger ref opened it). Hosted outside the row, as `FollowupPopoverHost` is: a write in it (a status update that follows the task up, a checked subtask or typed notes that move Modified) can take the row out of its list or group, and it stays open — anchored to the row's trigger while the row is there, where it was once it is gone (cr31 T1; `e2e/row-popover-host.spec.ts`). Keyed by kind + task; the row's popover level carried in the request; closed by a layout switch or the task's delete |
| TaskPickerHost | components/overlays/TaskPickerHost.tsx | Shows the task picker `ui-store.taskPicker` asks for (`openTaskPicker`): for a task (Make subtask of… → `services/subtask-convert.makeSubtaskOf`, which may ask first and closes the editor the task was open in) or for a subtask (Move to another task… → `subtask-store.moveToTask`). One host beside `ConfirmPromptHost`, so a row's menu, a search result's, the editor's footer and a checklist's ⋯ open the same dialog; it closes itself when what it was opened for is gone |
| ConfirmPromptHost | components/overlays/ConfirmPromptHost.tsx | Shows the oldest question in `ui-store.confirmPrompts` (`askConfirmation` — code that awaits a yes / no; `askChoice` — a third answer, `discard`, e.g. an editor's Save / Discard / Keep editing) as the shared `ConfirmDialog`, `nested`, with the prompt's `cancelLabel` / `discardLabel`; Cancel / Esc / backdrop answer false (`cancel`). A prompt with `cancelIsDefault` opens with focus (so Enter) on Cancel. Each question is keyed by its own `id` (a question queued behind leaves the one on screen alone); a run of queued questions returns the focus, once the last is answered, to what had it before the first. Mounted in `App.tsx` outside the shell (beside the sync-file prompts), so it also shows during startup. Asks "Continue without a backup?" (`stores/safety-snapshot`) and Export & delete's "Did the file save?" |
| UndoSnackbar | components/overlays/UndoSnackbar.tsx | Bottom-center toast after destructive actions with "Undo" button, auto-dismiss 5s; an entry pushed with an `action` shows its button (filled, `[data-snackbar-action]`) beside Undo — the checklist's **Complete task** — and taking it dismisses the toast; an entry with a `detail` shows it as a second, secondary line (`[data-snackbar-detail]`) and stays 8s — a checklist's last open subtask checked (`All 2 subtasks are done`); a **notice** (`useUndoStore.notify`) has no Undo, wraps, and stays 8s — the result toast of an operation that can't be undone (e.g. "Deleted 1 completed task. No backup was taken."). Mounted on desktop and mobile. Its message is announced through a visually hidden `role=status` live region that is always mounted (cr31 T13) |
| FilterSheet | components/overlays/FilterSheet.tsx | Thin mobile wrapper around `<FilterChipBar predicate={...} onChange={...} density="mobile" />`. The accordion sections (search, date range, toggles, people / tags / statuses) all live in the shared primitive at `density="mobile"` (its styles: `filters/FilterChipBarMobile.module.css`); the sheet contributes the bottom-sheet chrome + close button + reads/writes `useFilterStore` via `criteriaToPredicate` / `predicateToCriteria`. Its filter field (`Filter tasks`, placeholder `Filter…` with the token examples) is the search box (`useSearchBox`, written at once; chips only — the desktop field's expressions aren't typed here): tokens set the sections' values, Quick Add's popup opens inside the sheet under it (44px rows), the tint mirror lies over the input, a name matching nobody gets a note line. An expression typed in the desktop top bar shows under the field, read-only in words with its × (`ScreenExpression`). Autofocuses its search input when opened by `ui-store.openFilterSheetSearch` (the mobile workspace search button). While a screen filter is on it ends with a full-width accent **Show N tasks** (`useScreenTaskCount`; `data-show-filtered-tasks`): a tap closes the sheet and opens the screen filter's task dialog (`task-dialog.screenTaskDialog`); the backdrop's touch drag doesn't scroll the page (`hooks/use-block-touch-scroll`, attached when the sheet opens — the component stays mounted and renders nothing while closed; cr31 T10) |
| TaskDialog | components/overlays/TaskDialog.tsx | The task dialog (`ui-store.taskDialog`): the tasks behind a glance widget's number (or a Neglect map's Slipping list), a person's or tag's open tasks (a person's / tag's menu › Show tasks — a row's avatar / tag, a picker's option), or — on a phone — the screen filter's tasks (`Filtered tasks`, glyph `filter`: the Filters sheet's Show N tasks; desktop shows those in the top bar's `FilteredTasksPanel`, and a window turning desktop-sized closes that dialog): that dialog has `TaskDialogFilterBar` under its header. What it shows is `TaskDialogContent`. Standard dialog, 660 wide, max 80vh (the body scrolls). A `DialogFrame` on the `overlay` tier, so the rows' popovers / menus open above it; focus starts on the panel and comes back to it when the editor closes; Esc (also with nothing focused) closes it except while the editor is open (`escapeBelongsElsewhere`); a backdrop press, Tab wrap, focus return and the modal shortcut scope are the frame's. A row click opens the editor (`openEditPopup`); `TaskEditPopupHost` then stacks it `nested` over the dialog and the editor takes focus, so Esc closes only the editor (focus returns to the dialog). Mounted by `WorkspacePage`, rendered into `<body>` |
| TaskDialogContent | components/overlays/TaskDialogContent.tsx | What the task dialog and the filtered-tasks panel show (`TaskDialog.module.css`): source glyph + title + count badge (tasks, a task under two headings once) + the group · sort control (`GroupSortControl`, `data-testid=popup-group-sort`, at the header's end: the popup lists' view — `task-dialog.popupListView` over Settings' new-list defaults and `ui-store.popupListPick`; a pick re-resolves the rows, and is every popup list's after it until a reload) + ×, a person's actions (payload `personId`: `PersonActions`, a group `Actions for <name>` under the title — **Review** alone (named `Review with <name>`; closes the dialog, then `review-jump.jumpToReview(review.personReviewFilter(person))`; shown where `useEntityMenuOffers` offers Review — outside a dialog the task dialog can't show above) — a button with the accent `ReviewIcon`, D26 BD; the check-ins live on the Review widget's field), an optional `filterBar` slot, the description line (the screen filter's: `screenTaskDialogDescription`, following the filter), rows (`TaskRow` in a `TaskRowGrid` with the default split and no divider, `MobileTaskRow` on phones; no drag; headed groups get group headers), footer Copy tasks (`copyTasksRich` over `taskDialogCopySections`; `Copied` for a moment) · Close Esc (a secondary button); with `onReview` (the filtered-tasks panel) **Review these N tasks** beside Copy tasks, N the open tasks listed, each once (none with no task). The source resolves once when the payload arrives (`resolveTaskDialog` over the stores as they are), so a row completed here stays, struck through, and a row edited out of the set stays until it closes; deleted tasks drop out. The `screen` source alone subscribes to `filter-store.filters` and resolves again on every change of it — fixed between changes; every source resolves again on a new group · sort pick. Copy follows the rows as shown. When the editor on top closes, the focus goes back to the surface (`panelRef`) |
| ReviewRail · ReviewCard, ReviewCompactCard, ReviewFooter, ReviewUpdateLine, ReviewFocusOnOpen · ReviewTitleField · ReviewQueueButton · useReviewFollowUp · ReviewSessionContext, useReviewSession, useReviewSessionApi | components/review/ (ReviewRail.tsx, ReviewCard.tsx, ReviewTitleField.tsx, ReviewQueueButton.tsx, use-review-follow-up.tsx, review-session-context.ts, Review.module.css) | **The review's parts** (D26), the Review widget's (the review deck that shared them is gone), over the review session the host provides (`ReviewSessionContext`: `reviewWidgetSession(tabId)`; drawn outside one, a part throws). `ReviewRail` — `Sort: <name> ▾` (item 7; a `MenuPopover` of radio rows, `review-queue.setReviewSort`), staying put over the rows, which scroll under it; then the rows in `order`, **two lines each** (board BL: ✓ / –, the priority ring on its box — with `onToggleComplete` (the widget's rail and its folded queue) a checkbox laid over the box's place beside the row's button, `data-review-row-check`, named `Mark “<title>” complete` / `incomplete`, 24px to hit; without it a picture — and the whole title — `data-review-row-title`, cut only at the rail's width —; under the title, `data-review-row-dates`, the next follow-up after `FlagIcon` and the due date after the marker `ClockIcon`, in words and the rows' colour states — a follow-up today / late yellow, a due date soon / late magenta; no second line without a date; the current row: the accent's tint and a 2px accent edge on its left; a row followed up today dims its title), the source's dividers in Queue order only (keyed by task, so a kept current task doesn't shift them), the current row kept in view (the focus with it while in the rail), a click jumping without taking the focus, ← / → on a row Back / Skip. `ReviewCard` — the task layout's card (its header the box, the title — `ReviewTitleField`, a field edited in place as the editor's: ⏎ / blur write it, Esc puts it back; a textarea growing over a hidden copy of its text, on the medium face an input —, `Created … · Modified …` and a ⋯ with the editor header's actions: Open in editor, Duplicate, Make subtask of…, Delete; task layout B: the band, Subtasks and Notes beside Status updates; it fills its grid area, the columns scrolling inside; under 390px of card the band and the columns scroll as one) and the footer (`ReviewFooter` with `followup`: ‹ Back and Skip › at its left end — `data-review-steps`, last in the DOM and drawn first (`order: -1`), so the update line's Tab reaches Last, then Next —, the follow-up fields — `TaskFollowupFields`: the Last field and the card's Next parts — and Mark followed up ›; its Status update line carries `dom-contract.REVIEW_UPDATE_ATTR`, where a jump puts the focus); `ReviewCompactCard` — the widget's medium face (board BM): the box and title, a facts line (due · status · priority · next · last), then its updates dock (`data-review-dock`, the task layout's dock at this size: the **Status updates** heading — always there, the count beside it —, the latest two entries oldest first, read-only, the newest `review-newest-update`, the update line, then the Next row — `TaskFollowupFields` with the card's Next parts, `placement="row"`, `data-review-compact-next`; the quick intervals give way under 382px of card; in a short cell the older entry, the log, the heading, then the Next row give way — container queries on the card's height: 214 / 193 / 168 / 148px), the footer with Open card ↗ (`openEditPopup`; Last is edited there). `ReviewQueueButton` — **Queue ▾** while the queue is folded (task layout A: a widget under 820px): a button (`data-review-queue-button`, `aria-haspopup`, named `Queue`, its tooltip `Show the review queue`) opening `ReviewRail` with its sort in an `AnchoredPicker` under it (a `dialog` named as the queue; `data-review-queue-popover`; level 1 in a cell, 2 in the widget popup; Esc or a press outside closes it; a row picked closes it). `useReviewFollowUp(todo, flushNotes, updateRef)` — the update line's draft (`session.drafts`), the Next field (`useFollowupInput` from `nextAfterFollowup`; a typed date kept in `nextDrafts`), the Last field (`last`: `useFollowupInput(lastFollowupAt, 'last')`, its commit after the notes, ⏎ / blur written once), Mark followed up (notes first, `markFollowedUpWithUpdate` — one undo step with text —, the next card at once, the mark and the sent text cleared once written) and its tooltip (what it will do; the footer's hint went). Each card opens with the focus in its update line unless the rail has it or the host's `focusOnOpen` says no (the widget: only while the user works in it) |
| CheckinDialog | components/checkin/CheckinDialog.tsx, CheckinDialog.module.css | The check-in dialog, **Task check-ins** (`ui-store.checkinDialog`, opened by `openCheckinDialog(personIds)` — the Review widget field's Check-in, for the filter's people; mounted by the workspace page). A `DialogFrame` on the overlay tier, `min(980px, 100vw − 32px)` wide, two columns (under 760px one column — people, options, then the message — the body scrolling as one). **Left** (300px): **People**, a `listbox` (`aria-activedescendant`; ↑ / ↓ / Home / End and a click select; the selected row the accent's tint with a 2px accent edge) of `checkinRecipients` — the people it was opened for first, then everyone with an open task, by name —, each an avatar, the name, the count of their open tasks the filter keeps (`3 tasks`) and `· Copied ✓` once copied here; opened for people the first of them is selected, else the first row; `Nobody has open tasks assigned.` when empty. **Which tasks · remembered for every check-in**: the saved check-in filter as `PredicateChips` (stacked; no People field, no Completed dates; `All open tasks` while it narrows nothing), each edit `setCheckinTaskFilter` at once (the counts and the message follow). **Include**: Task notes / Other people on the task (dialog state, on — `CHECKIN_INCLUDE_DEFAULTS`). **Right**: `Subject` and its text with a **Copy subject** icon button (plain text, `copyCheckinSubject`); the selected person's message (`prepareCheckin`) in a sandboxed `iframe` (`sandbox=""`, a `default-src 'none'` CSP, white paper in either theme) titled `Check-in for <name>`; with no task for them, the frame's place says `No open tasks match Which tasks.` (`<name> has no open tasks.` with no filter) and Copy and Copy subject are off; **Copy** (primary, `Copy message`) puts the body on the clipboard as rich HTML + plain text (`copyCheckinMessage`, built afresh inside the click) — no follow-up, no undo entry. **Footer**: the status line (a polite live region: `Copied the check-in for <name>. Paste it into your message.`, `Copied the subject for <name>.`, or `Couldn’t copy to the clipboard. Try again.`), **Paste a reply…** (the reply import, closing this dialog), **Done**. Focus starts on Copy when the selected person's message has tasks, else on the people list; Copy keeps it; Esc / × / a backdrop press / Done close it and the focus returns to the opener (the Review widget's Check-in). |
| CheckinImportDialog | components/checkin/CheckinImportDialog.tsx | The check-in reply import dialog (`ui-store.checkinImportOpen`; `Paste a reply…` in the check-in dialog, or the Review widget field's Paste reply — which passes the person while the field names one, `checkinImportPersonId`). A `DialogFrame` on the same tier and with the same look as `CheckinDialog` (its CSS module `composes` the panel classes); the paste target takes the focus on open (`initialFocus`). Before a paste: a focused `textarea` paste target (`Paste the reply here (Ctrl+V)`) whose `onPaste` reads `clipboardData` `text/html` + `text/plain` (no clipboard permission; default insertion prevented) → `parseCheckinReply` → `diffCheckinReply` against every task in the todo store; empty paste / `too-large` / no task found → an error line, the target stays. After: summary (avatar · `Reply from <name>` — the footer name matched to a person case- and spacing-insensitively, else a required native `<select>` with a warning line — preset to the person the dialog was opened for, if any (`It’s taken as <name>’s reply — change it if someone else replied.`; the footer wins when it names one of your people) · `<n> of <m> tasks matched by Ref · <k> changes found` · `Paste again`); under it the footer note (`The person comes from the email’s “Check-in for …” footer, sent Oct 12.` — the send date is information only, never a gate: no timestamps are compared). One card (`region` named by the title) per task with changes or already completed in the app (`Already completed in the app. Nothing to apply.`): title + ref, the amber `Not assigned to <name>` line for a task the person who replied (the footer's, or the one picked — the ticks follow the pick: such tasks' changes unticked, the others as by default, a change ticked or unticked by hand kept as it is) isn't assigned to (its changes start unticked; a forwarded email can name any task's Ref), one row per change — fixed-size checkbox (`flex-shrink: 0`) · label (Completed / Status / Notes) · a preview of what applying does to the task now (every change starts ticked but emptied notes): `Mark complete` (a repeating task notes it moves to its next due date) / **New status update · today**, the reply's text under it, and `Newest now: MM/DD · text` when the task has one (`data-checkin-status-update`; a reply only ever adds an entry) / the notes line diff as text (`=` muted, `−` struck, `+` accent; each edited line — `diff.notesPreviewLines` pairs, in a run of removed lines then added lines, the lines keeping at least half the shorter one's words, by most words kept and in order; none when the line diff fell back past its LCS cap (`change.exact` false) — is one redlined `±` line (`utils/word-diff.wordDiff`: removed words `<del>` struck in `--color-danger-text` on `--color-danger-bg`, added words `<ins>` underlined in `--color-accent-text` on `--color-accent-bg-light` (both ≥ 4.5:1 on the card in either theme), kept words plain, a visually hidden `removed` / `added` in each mark); emptied notes add a warning); unticked rows dim. Unchanged tasks fold into one `No changes in N other tasks` line; unmatched refs list as `Not found: <ref> “<title>”` (or `Matches several tasks`), update boxes without a Ref as a skipped count. `Record a follow-up on these tasks` (on; its hint says what Mark followed up does to the next follow-up by the Settings rule — `today + 1 month`, or `cleared` while the automatic next follow-up is off). Footer: `You can undo this after applying.` · Cancel · `Apply N changes` (live; disabled until a person is known and ≥ 1 change is ticked) → `applyCheckinUpdates` → closes; the undo snackbar reads `Applied N changes from <name>`. Nothing pasted is rendered as HTML. Esc / × / a backdrop press / Cancel close it |
| SyncConflictDialog | components/overlays/SyncConflictDialog.tsx | Browser-vs-sync-file conflict prompt (shared `Dialog`): the two sides as selectable cards (a `radiogroup` "Version to keep" of native radios in labels, nothing picked at first; arrows move the pick) with each one's task count / unsynced-since or saved-at / schema version; Cancel (pause sync, also Esc) and one confirm named after the pick — `Keep browser data` / `Keep <file name>`, disabled (`Pick a version`) until a card is picked. Notes that the replaced side is backed up first (keeping the file asks "Continue without a backup?" if the browser backup fails) and that an older file is saved back in the current format |
| PurgeCompletedDialog | components/overlays/PurgeCompletedDialog.tsx | **The purge dialog**, opened from the storage pill's purge notice (**Review…**) — never at launch (shared `Dialog`, `blockBackdropClose`): count + scrollable title list of completed tasks past retention; **Not now** (initial focus, also Esc — deletes nothing), **Delete** (`purgeCompleted`), **Export & delete** (`exportAndPurgeCompleted`; picker cancel keeps the dialog open). Busy while either runs (Esc does nothing then); when nothing was deleted — the backup failed and the user cancelled, or a plain download wasn't confirmed — it stays open with a note. Deleting without a backup (the user chose Continue) shows a notice in the snackbar as well as the storage pill's Backup warning. Mounted by `PurgeNoticeHost` (`components/overlays/PurgeNoticeHost.tsx`, rendered by `App.tsx`): it reads the notice's uids (`stores/purge-notice-store`, offered at launch — `runAppStartup`, after the sync file's launch question and load — or after a sync-file load once launch's check began — `services/replace-data.onDataReplaced`, source `sync-file`; uids answered this session are not offered again) from the DB, again whenever the todo store reloads or the retention changes, and writes how many are still expired to the store (the pill's count; 0 hides the notice; nothing read while a sync-file prompt is open); while `reviewing` (Review…) it shows the dialog with those tasks and stamps `settings-store.purgePromptLastAt` as it shows (`recordPurgePromptShown` — the interval counts from the asking, whatever the answer; nothing is stamped while the notice merely shows, while it waits, or when nothing is left to list); the dialog closing in any way settles the notice (`closePurgeReview`) |
| AppendImportDialog | components/overlays/AppendImportDialog.tsx | **Duplicate picker** for Settings → Import tasks (append)…: lists every incoming task (duplicates first) with its match ("Already here (completed/active)" for uid, "Same title as an active task"); per-row Skip / Import as new (`aria-pressed`), "All duplicates: Skip / Import as new"; primary button "Import N tasks" |
| FileUpgradeDialog | components/overlays/FileUpgradeDialog.tsx | One-time consent before an older-but-supported sync file is loaded and rewritten at `CURRENT_DB_VERSION`; Cancel pauses sync |
| DatabaseTooNewDialog, DatabaseTooOldDialog, DatabaseChangedElsewhereGuard | components/overlays/DatabaseVersionDialog.tsx | The browser's database can't be used by this build; shown by `App.tsx` instead of the app, nothing to close (shared `<Dialog>`, `blockBackdropClose`); the newer one offers Reload only. `DatabaseTooNewDialog` — **Data from a newer version of TODO2**: saved by a newer build (`tooNewDatabaseMessage`; nothing read or changed), from `checkTooNewDB` or a launch that failed with `DatabaseTooNewError`. `DatabaseTooOldDialog` — **Data from an older version of TODO2**: below the schema floor (`tooOldDatabaseMessage` — the version found, when the browser can tell, and the older build that still upgrades it; nothing read or changed), from `checkTooOldDB` or a launch that failed with `DatabaseTooOldError`. Its ways on: **Download this data** (`data/old-database.readRawDatabaseExport` — the old data read raw, saved as `todo2-old-data-v<N>-<time>.json`, stamped with its own version for the older build), **Import a file…** (a file input → `readImportFile`; a file that can't be imported says why; one that can asks first — `Replace this browser’s data with <name> (N tasks)?`, the old data and its backups to be deleted, Download beside Cancel / Replace — then `replaceUnreadableDatabase` and a reload), or Reload. `DatabaseChangedElsewhereGuard` (mounted by `App.tsx` for the app's lifetime) — **TODO2 changed in another tab**: another tab opened the database at a newer version (a newer build after a deploy) or deleted it while this one had it open (`data/database.onDatabaseChangedElsewhere`); Dexie has closed this tab's connection and nothing it writes is saved any more, so the page is blocked with Reload only (cr31 data L3; `e2e/database-changed-elsewhere.spec.ts`) |
| QuickAddBar | components/task/QuickAddBar.tsx | Keyboard-first task capture surface; the entry point for new tasks. Its field is an input over a `TintedText` mirror; its popup is placed by `useNlpPopupAnchor` under the whole bar (`surfaceRef`), at the token's column — never over the chips or Details. Its `@ #` popup rows have the person / tag menu (`NlpPopup entityMenu`; Edit… · Delete… only — the bar is on the dialog tier, so the task dialog couldn't show above); a press in a layer stacked above the bar (that menu, the editor / confirm it opens) doesn't close it, and the open popup re-reads its rows when people / tags change. Top-anchored single-row bar on its own scrim (`Dialog.module.css` `blurScrim`: `--color-overlay` + the modals' `--backdrop-blur`, `MODAL_BACKDROP_ATTR` so a dialog opened over it adds no second blur, `data-testid="quickadd-backdrop"`; its own element, not a `DialogFrame` — the bar stays on the dialog tier, above the palette it can be opened from, and isn't `aria-modal`, since its popup and the layers it opens are portaled beside it): a press that starts and ends on it closes the bar (`useBackdropPress`, the frame's rule), and the backdrop takes the click, so nothing underneath gets it (a press elsewhere on top of the page closes it too). Opened via `Ctrl+Space` (`services/keyboard-shortcuts` → `useUIStore.openQuickAdd`), the top bar's New task button (desktop), the mobile FAB (`App.tsx`), the command palette's New Task (`command-registry`), or a list widget's "+ Add task" (`openQuickAdd({ defaults })`). Layout: 18 px title field (a `textarea`, `rows=1`, that wraps and grows with its text up to `TITLE_MAX_LINES` 6 lines, then scrolls; the title stays one line of text: ⏎ adds, ⇧⏎ breaks nothing, a pasted or dropped line break becomes a space), then the chips row, problem lines, and a footer (`@ person · # tag · ! status · / command` on the left; `Details Tab · ⏎ add · Esc close` on the right). **Token tints:** the field's text is transparent over an `aria-hidden` mirror (`data-testid="quickadd-mirror"`) with the same font, zero padding and wrapping (`pre-wrap`, `overflow-wrap: break-word`; no scrollbar on the field, so both have one width), scrolled with the field; the `@ # ! /` popup opens 4px under the field at its token's character in the mirror (`mirrorCharX`), on whichever wrapped line it is; the mirror wraps each span from `services/nlp-token-tints.tokenTints` in a `data-tint` span (tints use box-shadow, never padding, so characters line up; the token the popup is open on shows as a chip). **Chips** (task-row order): markers (Focus / Urgent / Important) › `#tags` › people (colour avatar; a new `@name` gets the dashed avatar) › due (`CalendarIcon` + `Fri 16`) › follow-up (`/followup` date, `No follow-up` for `/followup none`, else the dashed `<date> · default` chip — every new task gets the Settings default) › repeat (only with a due date: `Every Mon` / `Daily` / …) › status. **Chip ×:** every chip ends in a × (`tabIndex=-1` — Tab stays the Details hand-off —, mousedown `preventDefault`, so the focus and the caret stay in the input; named `Remove #work` / `Remove @Anna` / `Remove Urgent` / `Remove due date` / `Remove repeat` / `Remove status`, `No follow-up` on the default follow-up, `Use the default follow-up` on a set or `none` one — `Remove follow-up` while new tasks get no follow-up, when there is no default chip). The chips come from the text, so × edits the text (`services/nlp-chip-edit`): it cuts every token the chip came from — and a command's or the status's `Repeated:` copies — leaving one space; the default follow-up's × appends `/followup none` (→ `No follow-up`, whose × cuts it again); a caret after a cut moves back with the text (a layout effect puts it back after the re-render — a popup pick's caret too, so keys typed right after a pick land after it). A preset marker's × drops it from the preset for this opening (`QuickAddDraft.removedPresetMarkers`; `App` takes them out of the defaults on submit and on the Details hand-off — `utils/filter-defaults.withoutMarkers`); a caption has no ×. **Problem lines:** `New person(s): @… — created when you add the task`, then one line per `CommandProblem` (`Unknown command:` · `Can't read date:` (+ the grammar) · `Can't read repeat:` · `Unknown status:` (a `!name` naming no status) · `Repeated:` (`the first /due counts`, `the first !status counts`) · `Ignored: /repeat … — repeating needs a /due date`), except the one the open popup already shows. Adding still works with problems: unknown commands and unreadable arguments stay in the title. The wrapper `AppQuickAddBar` (`App.tsx`) subscribes to `ui-store.quickAddOpen` / `quickAddDraft` and threads `onSubmit` (`services/create-task.createQuickAddTask` with the defaults from `quickAddDraft.defaults` (a list's predicate) or the active top-bar filter, then `showRecentlyCreated`; resolves `false` when the task couldn't be stored, and the bar — which closes once a promise `onSubmit` resolves `true`, a second Enter meanwhile ignored — then stays open with the text as typed) and `onOpenFullEditor` (stashes `{ rawTitle }` — plus any `defaults` — on `ui-store.quickAddDraft`, flips `quickAddOpen=false` without clearing the draft, dispatches `openCreatePopup`; `closeEditPopup` clears the draft; the editor re-parses the raw text and seeds its fields). Autocomplete is `useNlpAutocomplete` with `commands: true`; `NlpPopup` is `createPortal`'d to `document.body` and placed by `usePopoverAnchor` against a `rect` anchor — the input's box from the caret column to its right edge (below at the trigger character, above when clipping, ending at the input's right edge when it would cross the window's; re-measured on scroll / resize, re-placed on its own size changes); moving the caret (`onSelect`) re-evaluates the popup. While the popup is open ↑ ↓ Tab Esc are its keys (Tab picks), and ⏎ picks only after ↑ / ↓ moved the active row (`enterSubmits`); otherwise **⏎ always adds the task**: the token the popup is open on is completed to its best match first (`completeForSubmit` → `nlp-autocomplete.bestMatchCompletion`; an unreadable or still partial argument stays in the title as typed). Text that is only tokens adds nothing and shows the `No title:` problem line until the text changes. **`Tab` and the visible `Details` button both call `onOpenFullEditor?.(draft)`** — same handoff path; the bar deliberately does not call `onClose` here (parent owns the close-vs-handoff sequencing). Title input carries `data-shortcut-scope="none"` so `use-keyboard-shortcuts` skips global shortcuts while typing. The `TaskEditPopup` create modal is reachable only via the Details / Tab handoff. `preset` (`QuickAddPreset`: flags + an optional `caption`; `App` builds it from `quickAddDraft.defaults` markers) shows the seeded markers as chips (each with a ×) — even before anything is typed — and the caption at the chips row's end. Esc, the backdrop or a press outside with text typed asks first (`askChoice`: Create task / Discard / Keep editing); a close after a create doesn't. |
| RecentTaskPill, findFlightRow, flyTaskHome, pickFlightTarget | components/overlays/RecentTaskPill.tsx, task-fly.ts, task-fly.module.css | **A new task flies home** (flair P4): before the pill's first paint (a layout effect, once per creation) `findFlightRow` looks for a list row or line showing the task (`[data-todo-id]` with a title, not the pill's own — `data-no-flight`) whose centre is in the viewport and topmost there (`elementsFromPoint`, looking through the creating surface — `FLIGHT_SEE_THROUGH_ATTR` on Quick Add's overlay and the New task editor's panel, and the backdrop just before it — and through the pill); found, `flyTaskHome` holds the row closed (height, min-height, padding 0) for 320 ms while a ghost of the title (`[data-task-flight]`) flies to it from Quick Add's place on the spring curve, then opens it (160 ms) and glows it in the accent (600 ms), and the pill is cleared unseen; not found, on a phone or under reduced motion, the pill as below. `e2e/task-fly.spec.ts`. | The just-created task (`ui-store.recentlyCreated`, set by Quick Add, editor create and a one-line note → task) as a live `TaskRow` (in a `TaskRowGrid`: the default split, no divider) in a pill at the Quick Add position and width (a set width: the row sizes its columns from it) and a × to dismiss it; fades after ~5 s, hover pauses the fade; mounted once by `App.tsx` |
| ReassignDialog | components/overlays/ReassignDialog.tsx | `ConfirmDialog` asking to move a task from one person to another — a list widget row dropped on another person's group |
| AuditIssueDetailDialog | components/overlays/AuditIssueDetailDialog.tsx | Read-only detail for one audit issue (Settings → Data integrity): the offending rows with the bad fields highlighted, values on one line each, and what cleanup will do (`describeFix` per fix kind — for `adopt-lists`: each list to the first layout that shows it, the rest into a new layout `Recovered lists`, `RECOVERED_LISTS_LAYOUT_NAME`). Cleanup runs from the section after it closes |

## Popover patterns

Floating panels anchored to a click-coord or a trigger element route positioning + dismissal through the shared `usePopoverAnchor` hook (`hooks/use-popover-anchor.ts` — see services-and-utils.md). The hook owns flip-on-clip + clamp + scroll/resize/Escape/outside-click; the caller owns the panel + its `createPortal` wrapper. Every popover is on it: `MenuPopover` (and with it `WidgetKindMenu`, `TaskMenu`, the layout picker, …; its submenus and panels too), `AnchoredPicker` (the row / mobile row / tag chip / bulk-bar pickers), `RuntimeFilterPicker`, `FollowupPopover`, the desktop `FilterChipBar` pickers and Filter menu, `StoragePill`, `CalendarDayPopover`, `EditListPopover` (rect anchor: its list's cell), `PredicateFieldPicker`, `SwitchToManualPopover`, `StatusEditor`, `TaskNotePopover`, `TooltipBubble` (`bottom-center`) and the `@ # ! /` popups of `QuickAddBar` and the notes (`NotesNlpPopup`) — both `rect` anchors at the caret. The one exception is the drop refusal tooltip, which follows the pointer and is kept inside its widget, not the viewport.

**When to add a new popover, do this and only this:**

1. Choose an anchor: `{kind:'ref', ref}` for an element-anchored popover (panel tracks the trigger on scroll); `{kind:'point', x, y}` for click-coord popovers (right-click / contextmenu).
2. Call `usePopoverAnchor({ anchor, open, onClose })`. Defaults handle scroll-close / resize-close / Escape / outside-click.
3. Render the panel inside `createPortal(<div ref={panelRef} style={style}>…</div>, document.body)` — the hook is the *only* place that should compute panel position; consumers should not also write a separate clamp / flip `useEffect`.
4. For nested flyouts that sit outside the panel (e.g. `MenuPopover`'s submenus), pass `extraInsideRefs: [flyoutRef]` so outside-click ignores them.
5. Ref-anchored popovers that should *reposition* on scroll instead of closing (`RuntimeFilterPicker`) pass `closeOnScroll: false, closeOnResize: false` — the hook's reposition mode kicks in for `kind: 'ref'` anchors, while `kind: 'point'` anchors stay static. `closeOnScroll: 'user'` (`FollowupPopover`) closes only on the user's own scrolling — a wheel or touch drag outside — not on a scroll the page makes itself.
6. Popovers that own special Escape logic (e.g. `MenuPopover`'s "close the submenu first, then the menu") pass `closeOnEscape: false` and keep their own keyboard handler. `consumeEscape: true` stops the Escape that closes a popover there (a row picker in the task dialog doesn't also close the dialog).
7. A panel whose size changes while open (a picker's list narrowing as you type, an editor growing) passes `observeSize: true`; an anchor that isn't the trigger (a whole row) passes `anchorIsInside: false` so a click on it closes the popover.
8. Make the panel's root a `PopoverRoot` (`components/shared/PopoverRoot.tsx`, a `div` taking every prop) instead of a plain `div`, and give its surface the popover tokens (`background: var(--color-popover-bg)`, `box-shadow: var(--shadow-dropdown)` — the ring is inside it, no border; D21). The root carries `data-popover-level` (`1` over the page, `2` on a popover or in a dialog — `popover-level.usePopoverLevel`) and provides its level to what it renders, so a popover opened from it — portaled or not — steps up; `tokens.css` swaps the tokens under `[data-popover-level='2']`. A popover whose sub-popovers render beside it rather than inside (`MenuPopover`'s submenus and panels, the Filter menu's pickers) wraps them in `PopoverLevelContext.Provider value={usePopoverLevel()}` itself.
9. To point at the anchor (D21), pass `caret: true` to the hook and its `caret` to the root (`<PopoverRoot caret={caret}>`): the hook keeps the gap at `CARET_GAP_PX` and returns `null` for a point anchor (nothing to point at) or before the panel is placed. The trigger shows the open look while the popover is open (`--trigger-open-ring`, `--color-trigger-open-fill`, `--color-accent-text`; with `:focus-visible`, `box-shadow: var(--trigger-open-ring), var(--focus-ring)`) — replacing any open tint of its own.
10. Nothing to do for layers opened over a popover: a press or an Esc in a layer stacked above it — a portal root later in `<body>` (`utils/dom-layers.isInLayerAbove`): a menu, a dialog or a confirm opened while it is open — isn't outside it, and an Esc with nothing focused while a modal is open above it (`hasModalAbove`) is that modal's. So a picker stays open behind the person / tag menu, editor or confirm it opened, and each Esc closes one layer (`e2e/picker-entity-menu.spec.ts`).

**What NOT to do:**

- Do not write per-component `useEffect` for `getBoundingClientRect()` math, scroll listeners, resize listeners, or document-level `mousedown` / `keydown` outside-click handlers. Every one of those is folded into the hook.
- Do not portalize a popover from the *call site* (`createPortal(<MyPopover/>, body)` at the consumer). The popovers above portal *internally* — adding a wrapping portal at the call site double-portals harmlessly but obscures intent.

## DnD diagnostic flag (`?debug-dnd=1`)

Append `?debug-dnd=1` to the URL to log every guard branch and resolution path of the task drag-and-drop path (list widget and glance-widget rows and calendar entries dropped on calendar days). When a drag silently misses (no console error, no visible feedback), flip the flag, redo the drag, and the console emits the ladder — collision rule chosen → resolved `over.id` → guard outcomes → final dispatch.

The flag is read once at module load via `utils/debug-flags.ts`; each call site uses the gated `dndLog(label, payload?)` helper so production console stays quiet when the flag is absent. This is **permanent diagnostic infrastructure** — the small bundle cost (a few dozen no-op string literals) buys real diagnose value.

**Wrap sites:**
- `utils/task-dnd/dispatch.ts` — every branch of `dispatchTaskDrop` (no-active-todo, calendar-day reschedule, calendar-day choice cancelled, a Planner tile or group, unhandled).
- `utils/task-dnd/collision.ts` — `buildTaskCollision` rule selection (no-active fallback, rule matched with index + algorithm + droppable counts, no-rule-matched fallback).

Workspace tab drags and list-row reorders don't go through these (they hit-test measured geometry) and are not logged.

## Settings Editors

| Abstraction | Location | Purpose |
|-------------|----------|---------|
| ThemeColorsEditor | components/settings/ThemeColorsEditor.tsx | Settings modal for the per-theme color overrides. Reads `useResolvedTheme()` on mount to seed a local `workingTheme: 'dark' \| 'light'` state and renders a Light/Dark segmented toggle (`role="tablist"` + `aria-selected`) at the top of the modal — switching the toggle flips which `colors[theme]` bag the inputs bind to *without* changing the user's actual theme preference (that lives in Settings → Appearance). Header copy reads "Theme Colors — <Working theme>". The twelve `colorLabels` keys (accent / appBg / surface / border / header / danger / warning / star / deadline, then the priorities focus / urgent / important — `--color-marker-*`) bind to `setColor(workingTheme, key, value)`; a priority with no override shows the color it follows (the accent, Danger, Follow up — `models/theme-colors.themeColorShown`), that one's override included, and stays following it until it is given its own (its own color also tints its typed token — `/urgent`, Quick Add's chip: `settings-store` sets `PRIORITY_TINT_VARS` with it); the Reset button targets only the working bag via `resetColors(workingTheme)`. Companion `ThemeColorsEditor.module.css` carries the toggle pill styling |
| PeopleEditor | components/settings/PeopleEditor.tsx | Editor modal ("Manage people…" in Settings → People, statuses & tags): list of people (sorted by name) with color swatch + name + initials; search filters by name or initials. Each row shows the person's task count, open and completed (`TaskCount` over `countTodosByEntity(assignedPeopleMap)`, after loading every task and its people — as `TagEditor`; the joins load again only when the set of tasks changes, `usePersonAssignmentsForLoadedTodos`, so a task edit doesn't render the editor). `editPersonId` opens it straight on that person's edit row, its name focused (a person's menu › Edit…, via `EntityActionHost`; saving that row closes the editor). Add / edit in place: name + initials (auto-generated from the name until edited) + optional color — `PersonColorField` = `shared/ColorInput` (shows `NEUTRAL_PERSON_COLOR` when unset) plus a Remove color × (`RemoveButton`) that clears it; each row's Delete is a trash icon named `Delete <name>`; a person without a color is saved without the key. Delete confirm (`EntityDeleteConfirm.DeletePersonConfirm`, shared with the row's avatar menu) shows the assigned-task count; `person-store.remove` is undoable Colour: the shared `ColorPicker` (the six preset hues, then the colour input). |
| EntityActionHost, DeletePersonConfirm, DeleteTagConfirm | components/settings/EntityActionHost.tsx, EntityDeleteConfirm.tsx | Mounted on `WorkspacePage`: `ui-store.entityAction` (a person's / tag's menu — a task row's avatar / tag, a people / tag picker's option) → Edit… = `PeopleEditor` on that person (name focused) / `TagEditor` on that tag, both `layer="nested"`; Delete… = the delete confirm — `Delete <name>? Assigned to N tasks.` / `N tasks currently tagged.` (counted in the database: `person-store` / `tag-store.countTasks`), Cancel focused, then the store's undoable `remove`. Everything here is on the `nested` tier, over any dialog or picker the menu opened from (the picker stays open behind and shows the change). The editors use the same confirms |
| TagEditor | components/settings/TagEditor.tsx | Settings modal mirroring `PeopleEditor`: list of `Tag` rows (sorted by name) with color swatch + name + assigned count (counted from `tag-store.assignedTagsMap` after `ensureAllLoaded` + `loadAssignments` for every task — again only when the set of tasks changes, `useTagAssignmentsForLoadedTodos`). Add flow: name input + `ColorInput` (defaults to `DEFAULT_ENTITY_COLOR`; duplicate names rejected case-insensitively). Edit in place: name + color, Enter saves / Escape cancels; `editTagId` opens it straight on that tag's edit row (a tag's menu › Edit…), and saving that row closes the editor. The count is `assignment-helpers.countTodosByEntity` (`TaskCount`, shared with `PeopleEditor`). Delete flow: `EntityDeleteConfirm.DeleteTagConfirm` showing the task count (from the database, `tag-store.countTasks`) — `tagRepository.delete` cascades `todoTags` rows inside a transaction and the top-level `tag-store.remove` wraps it in `undoable` (undo re-adds the tag row and the cascaded joins via `captureJoinRows` + `tagRepository.restoreWithJoins`). Search field filters the list. Rename is a single registry row update — no walking todos — so every consumer picks up the new name via the `assignedTagsMap` refresh. Tags created from projects (`Project: <name>`, schema v60) are ordinary tags Colour: the shared `ColorPicker` (the six preset hues, then the colour input). |
| EditorModal | components/settings/EditorModal.tsx | The Settings editors' modal (People, Statuses, Tags, Unused people & tags, Theme colors, Keyboard shortcuts, Quick-Add syntax): title + × (`DialogCloseButton`, `Close`) on a `DialogFrame` (`EntityEditor.module.css` `.modal` panel, `dialog` tier unless `layer` says otherwise — `nested` from `EntityActionHost`) — Esc, focus in / Tab wrap / focus back to the opener, a backdrop press, no app shortcuts inside. An inline row edit's Esc (`preventDefault`) cancels the edit and leaves the modal; the status icon picker consumes its Esc; `escapeBelongsElsewhere` (passed to the frame) keeps it open while Esc belongs inside — the Statuses editor's held row. Over Settings the section still registers it (`useStackedLayer`) |
| StatusEditor | components/settings/StatusEditor.tsx | Statuses modal (Settings → People, statuses & tags): rows with drag reorder (`useSortableRow` / `useSortableReorderHandler` → `status-store.reorder`; while a row is held, Esc cancels the drag, not the modal — `EditorModal`'s `escapeBelongsElsewhere`), color (`ColorInput`), icon (`StatusIconButton`: the status's `StatusMark` in its color, opening the shared `IconPicker` — Icons the status glyphs, `STATUS_GLYPH_CHOICES`; Emoji as everywhere, Show as, Recent; no No icon, J17; a glyph pick drops the emoji, an emoji pick keeps the glyph's key underneath — `models/icon.statusLookAfterPick`; the row's Enter / Esc ignore keys from the portaled picker), add / edit name, color and icon (written on Save / Add); delete confirms with the task count (`selectTodoCountByStatusId`; all tasks load when the editor opens) Colour: the shared `ColorPicker` (the six preset hues, then the colour input). |
| UnusedEntitiesDialog | components/settings/UnusedEntitiesDialog.tsx | Unused people & tags (Settings › People, statuses & tags › **Find unused…**, desktop and phone) on `EditorModal`: the help line, Ignore completed tasks, the People / Tags groups (they scroll; Remove N… stays below), Remove N… and its nested confirm — see the Settings sections row. Stays open after a removal |
| FollowupIntervalSetting | components/settings/FollowupIntervalSetting.tsx | Settings → Tasks › Task defaults: the **Set a next follow-up automatically** switch (`role=switch`, `settings-store.autoFollowup`, stored as `newTaskFollowup`) above the **Follow-up interval** (`1m`, `2w`, `10d`, `1y`): on, new tasks and every Mark followed up set the next follow-up to today + this; off, new tasks get none and Mark followed up clears it (`settings-store.autoNextFollowup`; the interval's help text says which) — either way Mark followed up keeps a next follow-up after today (cr25 P6, `utils/followup.nextAfterFollowup`; the switch's help says so). The interval commits on Enter / blur; invalid text reverts. Then **Quick follow-up intervals** (`settings-store.followupQuickIntervals`, the `followupQuickIntervals` row, absent at the default `1d 1w 1m 3m`): 1–6 intervals typed with spaces (`utils/followup.parseQuickFollowupIntervals`), the chips beside every next follow-up date (`FollowupQuickButtons` reads it), the task menu's Follow-up › and `/followup`'s rows; commits on Enter / blur, invalid text reverts |
| ShortcutList, KeyboardShortcutsModal | components/settings/ShortcutList.tsx, KeyboardShortcutsModal.tsx | Every keyboard shortcut with its live keys (the user's changes included), by category (`utils/shortcut-bindings.shortcutListSections`), in the `?` modal |
| ShortcutEditor | components/settings/ShortcutEditor.tsx | Settings › Keyboard shortcuts: every shortcut with its live keys; an editable one has **Change** (records in a focused `role=textbox`: a key combo saves at once; a plain key waits for a second plain key — a sequence like G then S — or Enter for the key alone; Esc cancels and keeps Settings open, Tab / a click elsewhere cancels) and, once changed, **Reset** (back to the default; the changed keys show in the accent colour). Keys `settings-store.setShortcut` refuses show the reason under the row (`role=alert`) and recording goes on. Fixed keys (Esc, the selection keys, Enter, the notes editor's) are listed without buttons. Inside the Settings dialog, so no app shortcut fires while recording |
| NlpSyntaxModal | components/settings/NlpSyntaxModal.tsx | The Quick Add syntax reference (`@person`, `#tag`, `!status`, the slash commands) as a modal, with a Search section (the search box's tokens, D16) |

## Shared Components

| Abstraction | Location | Purpose |
|-------------|----------|---------|
| FollowupPopover, FollowupQuickButtons, FollowupDateButton, MarkFollowedUpButton | components/shared/followup/ | Follow-up editing (the task editor's rows are `task/FollowupEditorGroup`). `MarkFollowedUpButton`: the one **Mark followed up** button (the popover's, the editor footer's, the review footer's) — the follow-up yellow, the flag-check icon, always that label; `doneToday` sets `data-done-today` and the quiet look (`dimmed` overrides the look), `fullWidth` (the popover), an `adornment` after the label kept out of its accessible name; ref, `disabled`, tooltip trigger props and `onClick` pass through. `FollowupPopover` (portaled, `usePopoverAnchor` with `closeOnScroll: 'user'`; a row's through `FollowupPopoverHost`, a `rect` anchor; the bulk bar's a `ref`): **a date or Mark followed up leaves it open**, the two being often done together — after a date, focus goes to Mark followed up (to the field when that is done today); after Mark followed up, to the field, its text selected, and the Status update line empties; so ⏎ ⏎ records and closes, `2w` ⏎ ⏎ ⏎ sets, records and closes (a held ⏎'s repeat isn't a second press). **However it closes, what was typed sticks** (an unmount cleanup, so the row's toggle and the layout switch too): a date in the field is set, a Status update line is Mark followed up with that entry; text that isn't a date holds Esc / an outside click once (its error adds `close again to discard it`), the second drops it. The Next input opens on what Mark followed up will leave (cr25 P6, `nextAfterFollowup` over `autoNextFollowup`): the task's own next follow-up when it is after today, else today + the interval (empty while the automatic next follow-up is off), and then the help line under the field says what it was (`Was today` / `Was Mon 11/02 · overdue` — the field's `aria-describedby`; there is no `Now:` text); the bulk bar passes `current` — the selection's shared value (treated as one task's), or `mixed`: the field opens empty with the placeholder `Each task’s own` and the help line `Future dates kept · the rest → Tue 10 Nov` / `… the rest cleared`; focusing the field — click, once per focus, or Tab — selects its text (`useSelectAllOnFocus`); opens with focus on **Mark followed up** (⏎ / click = last now; with the field **untouched** `onFollowedUp(undefined)` — the store's rule decides per task, a later date kept —, edited `onFollowedUp(date)` / `null` when emptied; text that isn't a date disables it with the field's error; its tooltip says what happens: `Record a follow-up today and keep the next follow-up` / `… and set the next follow-up to the date above` / `… and clear the next follow-up` / `Record a follow-up today (no next follow-up)` / mixed `…; future next follow-ups are kept, the rest set to <date>` / `… the rest cleared`) or, with `initialFocus: 'next'` (the row menu's "Pick a date or interval…"), on the Next input with its value selected; focus goes back to the trigger on close; live parse help, ⏎ in the field (sets the next follow-up only — when it differs from now; equal, or untouched on a mixed selection, closes) / a day from the date picker (opens on the field's day; sets the next follow-up only); a **quick interval** sets the next follow-up only, as a picked day does, × clears the next follow-up at once and the field stays empty, not back on the automatic date, so Mark followed up then leaves none (over text typed on a mixed selection it only erases the text); text deleted by hand writes nothing until ⏎ (the help line: `No next follow-up · ⏎ to clear`); last-follow-up line; after the quick intervals an optional **Status update** line (cr27, design S2: label `Status update`, `optional` at the right, `Add an update…`, 500 characters) with the task's newest entry under it (`newestUpdate`, `MM/DD text`, muted date; none for a task without one or a mixed bulk selection): with text, Mark followed up — its button (its label unchanged, its tooltip ending `, and add the status update`, undimmed though done today), or ⏎ in the line — calls `onFollowedUp(next, update)`, and the hosts (`FollowupPopoverHost` → `use-bulk-actions`, `ListBulkBar`) go through `services/mark-followed-up.markFollowedUpWithUpdate`: the follow-up and an entry dated today with `followedUp`, one undo step; empty = exactly the plain Mark followed up; stops React event bubbling to the host row. `FollowupDateButton`: the date picker of each field — a calendar button (keyboard-reachable, `Pick next follow-up date` / `Pick last follow-up date`) that opens the browser's picker on a hidden `type="date"` input (`showPicker`, focus as the fallback; optional `max`), as the due date's does. Opened from the row pill, the hover-revealed empty-slot flag (`TaskRow`), and the row menu's "Set follow-up…" (point anchor); `MobileTaskRow` opens it from its task menu's Follow-up › Pick a date or interval… (a hidden anchor at the title's start) |
| useNextFollowupField, followedUpWords, sameDay | components/shared/followup/use-next-followup-field.ts | The Next follow-up field beside a Mark followed up button, one for the follow-up popover and a review card (`review/useReviewFollowUp`; cr34 A4): the field starts on what Mark followed up leaves (`nextAfterFollowup` over Settings' automatic date; a mixed selection: empty), and reads back `fieldDate` (a date, none, `invalid`), `kept`, `differs` (⏎ has something to set) and `followUpNext` (what Mark followed up passes: none while untouched — the store's rule per task). `followedUpWords` is Mark followed up's tooltip (keep / set / clear; `nameDate`: the review names the date and says when the field doesn't read, the popover says `the date above`) |
| RollingNumber | components/shared/RollingNumber.tsx, RollingNumber.module.css | A figure whose digits roll (flair P1): a span per character; a digit that changes while mounted gets `data-prev` (the old digit, drawn by a `::before` with empty alt text) and `data-dir` (`up` as the number rises, `down` as it falls) and a fresh key, and slides in its own `clip-path` window (280 ms, the spring curve); nothing on mount, non-digits never roll, places line up from the right; its text is the plain figure. The Number's count and each shape's main figure (percent, age, goal count, group counts, a followed list's count) |
| BadgeCapsule | components/shared/BadgeCapsule.tsx | Due (magenta) and follow-up (yellow) counts in one capsule, each part solid while some of it is overdue (`TabBadgeCounts`), a part with 0 left out (`[data-badge-part]`). A list tab's badge (`CellTabStrip`, `role=img` named by its summary) and the top bar's counter / the phone's pill (aria-hidden: the button's name says it) |
| AvatarStack | components/shared/AvatarStack.tsx | Overlapping circle avatars with `+N` overflow (default max=3); each avatar is filled with `person.color`, falling back to `NEUTRAL_PERSON_COLOR` (no store subscription). `sm` variant; click bubbles to `onClick` (opens the people picker in TaskRow), right-click on a visible avatar surfaces per-person context menu. `readOnly` renders plain `<span>`s instead of `<button>`s — used by the search dropdown so avatars never intercept the row click. Consumed by `TaskPillPeople` (TopBar search rows) and `CalendarEntryButton` |
| TaskPillBar, TaskPillPeople, TaskPillDates, TaskPillFollowup, TaskPillStatus | components/shared/TaskPillBar.tsx | Shared task-pill primitive. `<TaskPillBar>` composes people → dates → follow-up → status, used by TopBar's `SearchResultRow`; the atoms are exposed for surfaces with their own layout — `CalendarEntryButton` uses `<TaskPillStatus>` (the desktop `TaskRow` has its own column cells; the phone row its quiet details line, UX cleanup 2C). The due pill (`Oct 20`) hugs its date (4px side padding) and its hover × overlays its right end, taking no width. The people group, due pill and follow-up pill carry `data-pill="people"` / `"due"` / `"followup"`; the follow-up label is its own span. `TaskPillFollowup`: yellow flag pill for `nextFollowupAt` (`Today` / overdue = stronger fill + 1px `--color-followup-glow` ring, no "late" text (the tooltip says `Follow-up overdue`, the accessible name ends `, late`); completed never overdue; × clears only the follow-up; two-line tooltip via `useTooltip`). Props: `todo` + pre-resolved `people` / `status` + `today` + optional `interactive` (default true; search rows pass false to render non-interactive `<span>`s so the row's click target stays unobstructed — since 2C no caller renders it interactive) + `compact` (icon-only date markers + smaller avatars) + `ariaHidden`. No `tags` prop. Perf invariant: the primitive does NOT subscribe to stores — every consumer threads pre-resolved data and resolves once at the surface boundary |
| ChipSelector | components/shared/ChipSelector.tsx | Lookup-or-create picker for people and tags: focused search (`combobox` + `listbox`, `aria-activedescendant`), a list with a ✓ per assigned item — the assigned items first (alphabetical; for the bulk bar the ones every selected task has), a separator, then the rest (alphabetical); the order is fixed on open and whenever the query changes (`usePickerOrder`), so a tick with the query unchanged never moves a row, and an item created since goes by its state (a new one shows ticked on top) —, case-insensitive substring match marked with `followup-bg`, and `Create "<query>"` last (after a separator when there are matches; the only, active row when nothing matches) unless a name matches exactly in any case. ↑/↓ move the active row (wrapping; an exact match starts active; the active row is kept by what it stands for, so it follows a re-sort), ⏎ toggles it or creates; a pick (click, ⏎) with a query typed clears the query — the whole list, re-sorted, the picked row still active (⏎ again undoes it), the focus in the search; Tab — while a query is typed — accepts it as the `@` / `#` popup does (assigns it, never unassigns a ticked one, or creates the Create row's; the query clears, the focus stays; with no query Tab moves on as usual, Shift+Tab always), Esc → `onClose`; the search's × (`ClearableField`) empties it; after a create the query clears and the picker stays open; a create that rejects says `Couldn't create "<name>".` in the snackbar (never swallowed). Focus returns to what had it before (a layout-effect cleanup, so a trigger revealed only while the picker is open can still take it). `avatars` = the person picker: 264 wide, a 16px avatar per row in the person's color, a dashed-ring initial on Create. `entityMenu` (`person` / `tag`; every host sets it): a right-click on an option, or Shift+F10 / the context-menu key in the search (a `contextmenu` without the right button) on the highlighted one, opens `PickerEntityMenu` for it — at the pointer, or under the option — without picking it (the option becomes the active row); not on the Create row; Esc closes only the menu and the focus is back in the search; Show tasks closes the picker (`onClose`). A right-click on the search keeps the browser's menu; no right-click in the picker reaches the host behind (a task row's task menu). Hosts: TaskRow / MobileTaskRow / TaskEditMetadata / ListBulkBar (people, rows from `utils/person.personPickerItems`) and TagChipSelector / TaskEditMetadata / ListBulkBar (tags) |
| EntityMenu, PickerEntityMenu, MenuEntity | components/shared/EntityMenu.tsx | The person / tag menu — one implementation for a task row's avatar / tag and a people / tag picker's option: a `MenuPopover` headed `@name` / `#name` (220px wide while it offers Review, which names the entity in full, else 180px) over `entityMenuEntries` (`person-menu-entries`): a person's Review with X · ─, a tag's Review #tag tasks · ─, then Show tasks (`personTaskDialog` / `tagTaskDialog`) · Edit… · ─ · Delete… (`ui-store.openEntityAction` → `EntityActionHost`), or for several entities (a row cell from the keyboard) one submenu each with the same entries. `onShowTasks` runs first in Show tasks and Review (a picker closes itself). Where (`useEntityMenuOffers`): Show tasks and Review are left out inside a dialog the task dialog can't show above (`useDialogLayer` not `null` / `overlay`: an editor, Quick Add); Review, a jump, is offered wherever Show tasks is — the phone too, with or without a Review widget (`services/review-jump`). No check-in items: the check-ins live on the Review widget's field. `PickerEntityMenu` takes a kind + id, reads the entity live from its store (closes itself once it's gone) and opens at a point with `restoreFocus` and `closeOnScroll`. `src/test/components/shared/entity-menu.test.tsx`; `e2e/row-entity-menu.spec.ts`, `e2e/picker-entity-menu.spec.ts` (at the pointer / under the option, Esc one layer at a time, the editor and confirm on top of a row / filter / Edit list / editor picker, the rename and delete shown live, Undo, Show tasks), `e2e/review-jump.spec.ts` (an avatar's Review with X jumping to another layout's Review widget; no Review item while none exists) |
| useEntityMenuOffers, EntityMenuOffers, personMenuEntries, tagMenuEntries, peopleMenuEntries, entityMenuEntries, entityName, personReviewLabel | components/shared/person-menu-entries.tsx | The one source of a person's (and a tag's) Review item and the person / tag menu (D26; widget feedback 2B), so `EntityMenu`, the People group header menu and the top bar's People filter footer read alike. Each Review item is a jump to a Review widget (`services/review-jump.jumpToReview`) with its filter (`services/review`): `personMenuEntries(person, { review, before?, after? })` = `Review with <name>` (`personReviewLabel`; `ReviewIcon`; `personReviewFilter`, `@Name`); `tagMenuEntries` = `Review #<tag> tasks` (`tagReviewFilter`); `peopleMenuEntries(people, opts)` = one person's `personMenuEntries`, several people's `Review N people’s tasks` (`peopleReviewFilter`, `@A or @B`) — each empty unless `review`; no trailing ellipsis (nothing asks), and no check-in items (decision 7: the check-ins live only on the Review widget's field). `before` / `after` run around each item's action. `entityMenuEntries(entity, options)` is the whole person / tag menu (above, then a separator, + Show tasks · Edit… · ─ · Delete…). `useEntityMenuOffers()` = `{ showTasks, review }` where the menu renders: Show tasks outside dialogs or on the overlay tier (`useDialogLayer`), Review wherever Show tasks is (a jump goes to the open layout's Review widget, else the popup review) |
| RemoveButton | components/shared/RemoveButton.tsx, RemoveButton.module.css | The app's one × for a pill's remove, a field's clear and a small remove (one × everywhere): a round button drawing `CloseIcon`, centred (inline-flex), quiet until hovered or focused (60 % opacity; full, on `--color-tint-8`, on hover / focus-visible), with a larger invisible target (`::after`, 24px round the 14px / 16px circle). `label` is required — its accessible name (`Remove Aisha`, `Clear due date`); `tooltip` sets `data-tooltip` (`true` the label, or its own text). `size` 14 (tight spots: Quick Add's chips, the filter pills, a row's date part, a task pill's due clear) · 16 (the default: the task layout's People / Tags pills, a list filter chip, a prompt row's chips and clear, a clearable field, the Review field, the Filter menu's rows, the task dialog's filter text) · 20 (the top bar field's Clear all filters, the task layout's due date, Settings' Remove color) · 24 (the follow-up fields' clear, beside their calendar button and drawn as it is through the host's `.inputClear`; a chart's count column's and a Number group's count's remove); `iconSize` where a host draws a larger box (the phone's 32px clears). `keepFocus` prevents `mousedown`'s default (the caret stays in its field); `stopPropagation` keeps the click from its pill or row; `as="span"` (`role="button"`, `tabIndex -1`) where the × sits inside another button (`TaskPillBar`'s due and follow-up clears). Every rule sits in `:where()`, so a host's `className` overrides any of it — a position, a fill over text (a row's date part, a Numbers strip slot's ×), `opacity: 0` until its pill is hovered. ESLint bans a bare text × in `src/components` (`eslint.config.js` `TEXT_TIMES`: JSX text, or an expression, that is only the character). |
| DialogCloseButton | components/shared/DialogCloseButton.tsx, DialogCloseButton.module.css | A dialog's, popover's or panel's close — the task editor header's ×: a quiet square drawing `CloseIcon` (12px), muted, `--color-tint-8` and the text colour on hover / focus-visible; `label` default `Close`, `tooltip` as `RemoveButton`'s. `size` 24 (a popover's or panel's header: the task dialog, the convert preview, Edit list, the Number / chart / Created vs completed editors, the calendar day popover, an empty slot's close in the layout edit mode, the undo snackbar's and the recent-task pill's Dismiss) · 28 (the default: the check-in dialogs, the Settings editors' modal, the expression Syntax sheet, the widget popup) · 30 (the task editor). Rules in `:where()`: a host passes the class its header's ⋯ uses so the two match (the task editor's `.headerButton`, the widget popup's `.action`), or a margin. | useTooltip, TOOLTIP_DELAY_MS, popoverOpenElsewhere | components/shared/use-tooltip.tsx | The app tooltip: `useTooltip(text, {shortcut?, labelLines?, prose?})` → `{triggerProps, tooltip}` (`prose`: a description in sentences, the bubble's `prose`, as Settings' ⓘ shows one). Opens after 400 ms of hover or keyboard (`:focus-visible`) focus — unless, when the delay ends, a popover is open (`[data-popover-level]`) over the trigger: one that doesn't hold it and doesn't come before (in document order) the popover that does (UX cleanup 1A: no row tooltip beside Edit list or a menu; popovers portal to `<body>`'s end as they open, so the one opened last — a choice menu from the popup lists' group · sort popover — is in use and its items' tooltips open); closes on leave, blur, press, Escape, scroll or resize (the last three through the bubble's `usePopoverAnchor`); touch (`pointerType` touch) never opens it. `text` lines split on `\n` (the first `labelLines` — default 1 — are the label, the rest muted; the Dates button's tooltip makes every type's line the label); while open the trigger is `aria-describedby` the `role=tooltip` panel (`shared/TooltipBubble`: portaled, `usePopoverAnchor` `bottom-center` — below the trigger or above when there's no room, centred, clamped; 180 ms fade once placed). `shared/Tooltip` wraps any content in a `<span>` trigger |

Disabled buttons show theirs too (Chrome sends them pointer events). The native `title` attribute is banned on DOM elements (ESLint `no-restricted-syntax`, an iframe's — its accessible name — excepted); an icon-only control that had only a `title` got an `aria-label`. Elements write `data-tooltip="…"` (DOM code too: the notes' convert button). |
